. The contribution of the term in the first line of (
2.29
) is easier to estimate, similar to (
4.11
) with
s=0
. Therefore

\|T^{II}_{1,j,k}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+2^{3m}\|T^{II}_{1,j,k}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}2^{-j}2^{-m+\delta m}\min\{2^{m},2^{-k}\}.

This suffices to prove the desired bounds (
4.7
).

Proof of (
4.8
).
The formulas (
2.26
) show that

(\partial_{t}+|\nabla|)T^{I}_{1,j,k}(t)=(-i)e^{it}\cdot R^{I}_{1,j,k}(t),

so the bounds (
4.8
) follow from the bounds (
4.6
) if
\ast=I
. Moreover, using (
2.29
),

\mathcal{F}\big\{(\partial_{t}+|\nabla|)T^{II}_{1,j,k}\big\}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-itv\cdot\xi}\frac{e^{it}}{1-i\mathcal{D}}(\partial_{t}\widehat{L_{1,j,k}})(\xi,v,t)\,dv.

Using (
3.30
), Lemma
3.6
, and (
4.2
), if
(j,k,m)\in A^{II}
, we have

\begin{split}\|(\partial_{t}+|\nabla|)&T^{II}_{1,j,k}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+2^{3m}\|(\partial_{t}+|\nabla|)T^{II}_{1,j,k}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\|(\partial_{t}L_{1,j,k})(t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}+\|(\partial_{t}L_{1,j,k})(t)\|_{X_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}2^{-j}2^{-m+\delta m},\end{split}

This gives the desired bounds (
4.8
) if
\ast=II
, which completes the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

5.
Bounds on the static terms and the type-I reaction term

In this section we estimate the
B_{T}
-norms of the static terms, which consist of the type-I components
R_{2,j,k}^{I}
defined in (
2.31
) and the type-II components
R_{2,j,k}^{II}
defined in (
2.33
). Moreover, the estimate of the type-I oscillatory terms
T_{2,j,k}^{I}
will be obtained as a byproduct.

It turns out that many error terms arising not only in this section but also in the next one fit into a general framework of trilinear operators. We define these next and then record some of the relevant estimates. We note that these bounds improve over crude estimates through the use of the density decomposition (
2.3
) into static and oscillatory components.

Assume that
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,1]
and
s\in[0,T]
, and define the trilinear operators

\begin{split}&\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(f,g;C)(x,\gamma,\tau,s):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(x-y,v,\tau,s)C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\\
&\times f(y-(s-\tau)v+P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s),\tau)g(y-(s-\gamma)v+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s),\gamma)\,dydv,\end{split}

(5.1)

where
\gamma,\tau\in[0,s]
,
(j,k)\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\times\mathbb{Z}
. We assume that kernel
\mathcal{K}_{j,k}
and the functions
P_{1},P_{2}
satisfy the uniform estimates

\begin{split}&\big|\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(y,v,\tau,s)\big|\lesssim 2^{3k}(1+2^{k}|y|)^{-8}\cdot 2^{-3j}\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-4,j+4]}(v),\\
&\big|\partial_{y}P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s)|+\langle s\rangle^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s)|\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},\\
&\big|\partial_{y}P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)|+\langle s\rangle^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)|+\big|\partial_{\gamma}P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)|\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}.\end{split}

(5.2)

Notice that we suppress the dependence on the kernels
\mathcal{K}_{j,k}
and the exact functions
P_{1},P_{2}
in the notation for the operators
\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}
. In most of our applications
P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s)=\theta_{1}\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)
and
P_{2}=\theta_{2}\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)
,
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in[0,1]
, and the bounds (
5.2
) follow from Lemma
3.8
.

We assume that the coefficient
C
is differentiable in
\gamma
, and define

\begin{split}\Lambda(C)(\gamma,\tau,s)&:=\|C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y,v}}+\|\langle\gamma\rangle\partial_{\gamma}C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y,v}}.\end{split}

(5.3)

Assume that
m_{2}\geq 0
,
t_{3},t_{4}\in[2^{m_{2}}-1,2^{m_{2}+1}]\cap[0,s]
, and define the trilinear operators

\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(f,g;C)(x,\tau,s)=\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(f,g;C)(x,\gamma,\tau,s)\,d\gamma.

(5.4)

For these we have the following bounds:

Lemma 5.1

(Trilinear estimates)
.

Let
s\in[0,T]
,
\tau\in[0,s],

2^{m_{2}-1}\leq s
. With the assumptions of the bootstrap Proposition
2.4
, and the notation and assumptions above, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\nabla E,\mathcal{R}^{2}E;C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\}\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s),

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}E,\mathcal{R}^{2}\nabla E;C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\}\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s),

(5.6)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}E,\mathcal{R}^{2}E;C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-0.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-0.1}\}\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s),

(5.7)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\nabla E,\mathcal{R}^{2}\nabla E;C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-2.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-2.1}\}\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s),

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\nabla^{2}P_{k_{1}}E,\mathcal{R}^{2}E;C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\}\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s),

(5.9)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\nabla^{2}P_{k_{1}}E,\mathcal{R}^{2}\nabla E;C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1}\min\{2^{-2.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-2.1}\}\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s),

(5.10)

for any
k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
, where
\Lambda^{\ast}(C)(\tau,s):=\sup_{\gamma\in[t_{3},t_{4}]}\Lambda(\gamma,\tau,s)
and
\mathcal{R}^{1},\mathcal{R}^{2}
are operators defined by Hörmander-Michlin multipliers satisfying the inequalities

\sup_{|\alpha|\leq 8}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\xi|^{|\alpha|}|D^{\alpha}_{\xi}\mathcal{R}^{l}(\xi)|\leq 1,\qquad l\in\{1,2\}.

(5.11)

Moreover, for any
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\gamma,\tau\in[0,s]
,

\begin{split}&\|\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho;C)(.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\langle s\rangle^{3}\|\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho,P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho;C)(.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}\frac{\min\{2^{k_{\min}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1}\}^{3}}{\langle\gamma\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{2}}+\langle\gamma\rangle^{-\delta}}\|C(.,.,.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,y,v}}.\end{split}

(5.12)

Proof.

For the sake of presentation we postpone the proof to section
7
.
∎

In the rest of this section we fix
t\in[0,T]
and an integer
m\geq 0
such that
t\in[2^{m}-1,2^{m+1}]
. We also fix an integer
K
such that

1\leq\varepsilon_{1}2^{K}\leq 8.

(5.13)

This is needed in some cases of very high frequencies, to ensure that the overall contribution is nonlinearly small. The formulas (
2.23
), (
2.24
), and (
3.1
) show that

\begin{split}L_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)&=P_{k}\big\{E(x,s)M^{\prime}_{j}(v)-E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))\big\},\\
\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)&:=\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),\quad\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t):=\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t).\end{split}

(5.14)

5.1.
Large velocity case:
j\geq 19m/20
or
m\leq\delta^{-4}

The goal of this section is to prove that the contribution of large velocities is acceptable.

Proposition 5.2
.

Assume that the bounds (
2.41
) hold,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
2^{j}\gtrsim 2^{19m/20}
. Then

\begin{split}\|\langle t\rangle^{1-\delta^{2}}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle R^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{-\delta^{2}j},\\
\|\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{1-\delta}\nabla_{x,t}T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{-\delta^{2}j}.\end{split}

(5.15)

Proof.

The first inequality follows from Lemma
5.3
and Lemma
5.4
below. To prove the second estimate in (
5.15
), it thus suffices to show that

\begin{split}\|\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}T^{I}_{2,j,k}\|_{B_{t}^{0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{1-\delta}\nabla_{x}T^{I}_{2,j,k}\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{-\delta^{2}j}.\end{split}

(5.16)

From (
2.31
) we have

\begin{split}T^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,t)&:=-i\int_{0}^{t}e^{is}e^{-(t-s)|\nabla|}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,s)ds.\end{split}

(5.17)

Notice that,

\begin{split}\text{ for any }p\in[1,\infty]\text{ and }\lambda>0,\qquad\||\nabla|e^{-\lambda|\nabla|}f\|_{L^{p}}&\lesssim\lambda^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{p}}.\end{split}

(5.18)

Using Lemma
5.3
, Lemma
5.4
, and (
5.18
), we find that

\begin{split}\|e^{is}e^{-(t-s)|\nabla|}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,s)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}\langle s\rangle^{\delta^{2}-1}2^{-\delta^{2}j},\\
\|e^{is}e^{-(t-s)|\nabla|}\nabla_{x}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,s)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{k^{-}}[1+2^{k}(t-s)]^{-1}\langle s\rangle^{\delta^{2}-1}2^{-\delta^{2}j}.\end{split}

This gives (
5.16
), upon integration.
∎

It remains to prove Lemmas
5.3
and
5.4
. We use the formula (
2.31
), thus

R^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}L_{2,j,k}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)\,dvds.

(5.19)

We start with the contribution of high frequencies.

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that
2^{j}\gtrsim 2^{19m/20}
. Then

\|\langle t\rangle\nabla_{x}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{T}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{-\delta j}.

Proof.

We examine the formula (
5.14
) and decompose, for
p\in\{1,2,3\}
,

\partial_{x^{p}}L_{2,j,k}:=P_{k}[Err_{j}]+\sum_{k_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}P_{k}[\mathcal{L}_{j,k_{1}}],\\

(5.20)

where, with
E_{k_{1}}:=P_{k_{1}}E
, we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{j,k_{1}}(x,&v,s,t):=\big[\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)-\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\big]\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\\
Err_{j}(x,&v,s,t):=\partial_{x^{p}}E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot\big[M^{\prime}_{j}(v)-M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))\big]\\
&-\partial_{x^{p}}\widetilde{Y}^{q}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)\partial_{x^{q}}E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))\\
&-E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot\big[\partial_{x^{p}}\widetilde{W}_{\#}^{q}(x,v,s,t)\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))\big].\end{split}

(5.21)

We first estimate the contribution of the error term
Err_{j}
. We expand as in (
3.2
)

\begin{split}\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)&=-\int_{s}^{t}E(x-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\gamma,t),\gamma)\,d\gamma,\\
\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)&=\int_{s}^{t}(\gamma-s)E(x-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\gamma,t),\gamma)\,d\gamma.\end{split}

(5.22)

Then we observe that, with the notation of (
5.1
), we can write the contribution of
Err_{j}
as a sum of terms of the form

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}[Err_{j}](x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dvds=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E,E,C^{1})(x,\gamma,s,t)d\gamma dsd\theta\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E,\nabla E,C^{2})+\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E,\nabla E,C^{3})\big](x,\gamma,s,t)d\gamma ds,\\
\end{split}

(5.23)

for suitable kernels
\mathcal{K}_{j,k}^{i}
and

\begin{split}C^{1}(x,y,v,\gamma,s,t)&=2^{3j}\nabla_{v}M_{j}^{\prime}(v+\theta\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)),\\
C^{2}(x,y,v,\gamma,s,t)&=2^{3j}(\gamma-s)(\delta_{p}^{\ell}+\partial_{x^{p}}\widetilde{Y}^{\ell}(y-tv,v,\gamma,t))M_{j}^{\prime}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)),\\
C^{3}(x,y,v,\gamma,s,t)&=2^{3j}(\delta_{p}^{\ell}+\partial_{x^{p}}\widetilde{Y}^{\ell}(y-tv,v,\gamma,t))\nabla_{v}M_{j}^{\prime}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)).\end{split}

Notice that
\Lambda(C^{1})(\gamma,s,t)+\Lambda(C^{3})(\gamma,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-2j}
and
\Lambda(C^{2})(\gamma,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-2j}\langle\gamma\rangle
. This leads to acceptable contributions using Lemma
5.1
and the bound
2^{j}\gtrsim 2^{19m/20}
.

Now, we move on to the estimate of the main contribution. We split into two cases based on the size of
k_{1}
as follows (recall
K
defined in (
5.13
)).

Case
\mathbf{1}
:
k_{1}\geq-m/2+K/2
. We first consider the
L^{1}_{x}
-estimate. Using Lemma
3.1
(i),

\begin{split}\langle t\rangle\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}&\mathcal{L}_{j,k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dvds\Big\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle 2^{-2j}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla_{x}E_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle 2^{-2j-k_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}ds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-2j-k_{1}}\langle t\rangle^{1+2\delta}.\end{split}

(5.24)

Thus the contribution for all
k_{1}
with
k_{1}\geq-m/2+K/2
is acceptable, due to the assumption
2^{2j}\gtrsim 2^{19m/10}
. Now, we consider the
L^{\infty}_{x}
-estimate.
If
t/2\leq s\leq t
, we use that

\begin{split}\|\nabla E_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-4}2^{-k_{1}}\end{split}

and integrate over
v
, so

\begin{split}&\langle t\rangle^{4}\Big\|\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}_{j,k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dvds\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{4}\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\nabla E_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}|M^{\prime}_{j}(v)|dvds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{2\delta+1}2^{-k_{1}-2j}\end{split}

which also gives an acceptable contribution. It remains to consider the case
0\leq s\leq t/2
. If
t\lesssim 1
then the same bounds as above hold. On the other hand, if
t\geq 10
then we can use the characteristics bounds in Lemma
3.8
to verify that the Jacobian of the map

w\mapsto y:=x-(t-s)w+\widetilde{Y}(x-tw,w,s,t)

is of size
\langle t\rangle^{3}
. Using a simple change of variable, we deduce that, for fixed
x
,

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v,s)M_{j}^{\prime}(v)|dv&\lesssim 2^{-5j}\langle t\rangle^{-3}\|P_{k_{1}}\rho(s)\|_{L^{1}},\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,s,t),s)M_{j}^{\prime}(v)|dv&\lesssim 2^{-5j}\langle t\rangle^{-3}\|P_{k_{1}}\rho(s)\|_{L^{1}}.\end{split}

Therefore

\begin{split}\langle t\rangle^{4}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}_{j,k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dvds\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-k_{1}-5j}\langle t\rangle^{2\delta+1},\end{split}

which again gives an acceptable contribution.

Case
\mathbf{2}
:
k_{1}\leq-m/2+K/2
. For this case, the derivative in
E
is not problematic. Note that, after taking the difference between the two electric fields, we have

\big[\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)-\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\big]M^{\prime}_{j}(v)=-\int_{0}^{1}L^{\theta}_{j,k_{1}}(x,v,s,t)d\theta,

(5.25)

where

L^{\theta}_{j,k_{1}}(x,v,s,t):=\widetilde{Y}^{q}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)\partial_{x^{p}}\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\theta\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v).

We expand the function
\widetilde{Y}_{\#}
as in (
5.22
). Therefore, for any fixed
\theta\in[0,1]
, in terms of the trilinear operators defined in (
5.1
), we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}L^{\theta}_{j,k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dv=\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E_{k_{1}},E;C^{4})(x,\tau,s,t)d\tau,

with
C^{4}(x,y,v,\gamma,s,t):=2^{k_{1}}(\gamma-s)2^{3j}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)
,
\Lambda(C^{4})(\gamma,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-2j}2^{k_{1}}\langle\gamma\rangle
. This leads to an acceptable contribution using Lemma
5.1
and recalling the assumption
2^{2j}\gtrsim 2^{1.9m}
.
∎

The following lemma deals with the contributions of low frequencies, when
\langle\nabla\rangle\approx 1
.

Lemma 5.4
.

If
2^{j}\gtrsim 2^{19m/20}
and
k\leq 0
then

\|R^{I}_{2,j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{0.9}2^{-2j}.

(5.26)

Proof.

We decompose

L_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)=L_{2,j,k}^{a}+L_{2,k,j}^{b},

(5.27)

where

\begin{split}L_{2,j,k}^{a}(x,v,s,t)&:=P_{k}\big\{E(x,s)-E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\big\}\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\\
L_{2,j,k}^{b}(x,v,s,t)&:=P_{k}\big\{E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot\big[M^{\prime}_{j}(v)-M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))\big]\big\}.\end{split}

We can rewrite

\begin{split}E(x,s)-E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)&=-\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)\cdot\nabla_{x}E(x+\theta\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)d\theta,\\
M^{\prime}_{j}(v)-M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))&=-\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t)\cdot\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\theta\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))d\theta,\\
\end{split}

We use the formulas (
5.22
), so for any
\ast\in\{a,b\}
we have

\begin{split}L^{\ast}_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)&=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{\ast,\theta}_{2,j,k}(x,v,\gamma,s,t)\,d\gamma d\theta,\\
\mathcal{L}^{a,\theta}_{2,j,k}(x,v,\gamma,s,t)&:=P_{k}\big[-(\gamma-s)E^{p}(x-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\gamma,t),\gamma)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\partial_{x^{p}}E(x+\theta\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big],\\
\mathcal{L}^{b,\theta}_{2,j,k}(x,v,\gamma,s,t)&:=P_{k}\big[E^{p}(x-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\gamma,t),\gamma)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times E(x+\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(x,v,s,t),s)\cdot\partial_{v^{p}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\theta\widetilde{W}_{\#}(x,v,s,t))\big].\end{split}

(5.28)

Therefore, for any
\theta\in[0,1]
, in terms of the trilinear operator defined in (
5.1
), we can write

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}^{a,\theta}_{2,j,k}(x-(t-s)v,v,\gamma,s,t)dv=\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E,E;C^{a})(x,\gamma,s,t),\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}^{b,\theta}_{2,j,k}(x-(t-s)v,v,\gamma,s,t)dv=\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E,\nabla E;C^{b})(x,\gamma,s,t),\end{split}

(5.29)

where

C^{a}(x,y,v,\gamma,s,t)=(\gamma-s)2^{3j}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\qquad C^{b}(x,y,v,\gamma,s,t)=2^{3j}\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\theta\widetilde{W}_{\#}(y,v,s,t)).

Clearly
\Lambda(C^{a})(\gamma,s,t)+\Lambda(C^{b})(\gamma,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-2j}\langle\gamma\rangle
. Therefore, using Lemma
5.1
,

\begin{split}\Big\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}^{\ast,\theta}_{2,j,k}(x-(t-s)v,v,\gamma,s,t)\,dvd\gamma\Big\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-0.1}2^{-2j},\end{split}

for
\ast\in\{a,b\}
. We integrate in
s\in[0,t]
and
\theta\in[0,1]
to prove the desired bounds (
5.26
).
∎

5.2.
Small velocity case:
j\leq 19m/20
and
m\geq\delta^{-4}

In this subsection we estimate the terms
R^{I}_{2,j,k}
and
T^{I}_{2,j,k}
in the case where
j\leq 19m/20
,
m\geq\delta^{-4}
, and
j+k\geq-\delta m/3
, and the terms
R^{II}_{2,j,k}
when
j\leq 19m/20
,
m\geq\delta^{-4}
, and
j+k\leq-\delta m/3
.

Since in the expression for
R^{II}_{2,j,k}
we gain a factor of
\mathcal{D}^{2}
over
R^{I}_{2,j,k}
, we can treat both these terms in the same way.
For any
\ast\in\{I,II\}
, we localize the size of
t-s
and decompose

\begin{split}R^{\ast}_{2,j,k}&=\sum_{0\leq n\leq m+2}R^{\ast}_{2,j,k,n},\qquad R^{\ast}_{2,j,k,n}(x,t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{n}(t-s)L^{\ast}_{2,j,k}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dsdv,\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&L^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t):=L_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t),\\
&L^{II}_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}_{j,k}^{II}(y,v)L_{2,j}(x-y,v,s,t)dy,\end{split}

(5.30)

and the kernels
\mathcal{K}^{II}_{j,k}(y,v)
are defined when
j\leq 19m/20
,
m\geq\delta^{-4}
, and
j+k\leq-\delta m/3
by

\begin{split}&\mathcal{K}^{II}_{j,k}(y,v):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iy\cdot\xi}\frac{\mathcal{D}(\xi,v)^{2}}{1+\mathcal{D}(\xi,v)^{2}}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\,d\xi,\\
&|\mathcal{K}^{II}_{j,k}(y,v)|\lesssim 2^{3k+2(k+j)}(1+2^{k}|y|)^{-100}.\end{split}

(5.31)

Therefore, we can think that the functions
L^{I}_{2,j,k}
and
L^{II}_{2,j,k}
differ essentially by a factor of
2^{2k+2j}
,
L^{II}_{2,j,k}\sim 2^{2k+2j}L^{I}_{2,j,k}
.

Our main result in this section is the following:

Proposition 5.5
.

Assume that the bootstrap bounds (
2.41
) hold, and
m\geq\delta^{-4}
,
j\in[0,19m/20]
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
n\in[0,m+2]
. Then

\begin{split}&\text{ if }\,\,j+k\geq-\delta m/3\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\|\langle t\rangle\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle R^{I}_{2,j,k,n}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{-(j+k^{-})}2^{\delta m/2},\\
&\text{ if }\,\,j+k\leq-\delta m/3,\text{ then }\,\,\|\langle t\rangle R^{II}_{2,j,k,n}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{\delta m/2}.\end{split}

(5.32)

In particular, for any
k,m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
m\geq\delta^{-4}
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{j\in[0,19m/20],\,j\geq-k-\delta m/3}\|\langle t\rangle^{1-\delta}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle R^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2},\\
&\sum_{j\in[0,19m/20],\,j\leq-k-\delta m/3}\|\langle t\rangle^{1-\delta}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle R^{II}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2},\\
&\sum_{j\in[0,19m/20],\,j\geq-k-\delta m/3}\|\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}+\|\langle t\rangle^{1-\delta}\nabla_{x,t}T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}.\\
\end{split}

(5.33)

Proof.

The basic bounds (
5.32
) follow from Lemma
5.6
, Lemma
5.7
, and Lemma
5.8
below. Here we show that they imply the bounds (
5.33
).

The first two bounds in (
5.33
) follow by summation with respect to
j
and
n
(notice that
k\leq 0
in the bounds concerning
R^{II}_{2,j,k}
). For the last bound, using (
5.17
), we have

\begin{split}\|e^{is}e^{-(t-s)|\nabla|}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(s)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}&\lesssim\|R^{I}_{2,j,k}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}},\\
(t-s)\|e^{is}|\nabla|e^{-(t-s)|\nabla|}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}&\lesssim\|R^{I}_{2,j,k}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}},\end{split}

for
s\in[0,t]
. Therefore, using (
5.17
),

\begin{split}\|T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{3\delta m/5}2^{-(j+k^{-})},\\
\||\nabla|T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}&\lesssim\int_{0}^{t-1}\|R^{I}_{2,j,k}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\frac{ds}{t-s}+2^{k}\int_{t-1}^{t}\|R^{I}_{2,j,k}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{-(j+k^{-})}2^{\delta m/2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta m/10}.\end{split}

Finally, we notice that
\left(\partial_{t}+|\nabla|\right)T^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)=-ie^{it}R^{I}_{2,j,k}(t)
, and the bounds in the last line of (
5.33
) follow by summation over
j
.
∎

We bound first the contributions of the small indices
n\in[0,10]
.

Lemma 5.6
.

With the hypothesis of Proposition
5.5
, if
n\in[0,10]
then

\begin{split}&\text{ if }\,\,\,j+k\geq-\delta m/3\,\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\,2^{j}\|\langle t\rangle\nabla_{x}R^{I}_{2,j,k,n}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2},\\
&\text{ if }\,\,j+k\leq-\delta m/3\,\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\,2^{j}\|\langle t\rangle\nabla_{x}R^{II}_{2,j,k,n}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{3/2}2^{j+k}.\end{split}

(5.34)

Proof.

To prove the bounds on
R^{I}_{2,j,k,n}
we use again the decomposition (
5.20
). The error terms can be written as in (
5.23
). The integrals over
s
and
\gamma
are over intervals of length
\lesssim 1
, so these contributions are easily acceptable using the bounds (
5.12
).

To bound the main term, we consider again two cases. For
k_{1}\geq m/4+K/2
, with
K
defined in (
5.13
), for
\theta\in\{0,1\}
we find that

\begin{split}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x^{p}}E_{k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v+\theta\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,s,t),s)\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v)dv\Big\|_{B^{0}_{s}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-k_{1}-2j}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}\end{split}

and each term in
\mathcal{L}_{j,k_{1}}
gives an acceptable contribution separately.

If
k_{1}\leq m/4+K/2
, we work with the difference of the two electric fields as in (
5.25
). As
t\geq s\geq t-2^{11}
, from rough bilinear estimates, we have

\begin{split}\Big\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}L^{\theta}_{j,k_{1}}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dv\Big\|_{B_{t}^{0}}&\lesssim 2^{-2j}\|\widetilde{Y}_{\#}(s,t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}E_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}2^{\delta k_{1}^{-}}\langle t\rangle^{-11/10}.\end{split}

The desired bounds follow by summation over
k_{1}\leq m/4+K/2
and integration over
s
.

The bounds on
R^{II}_{2,k,j,n}
are similar, using the identities (
5.30
)–(
5.31
) (recall the heuristical equivalence
L^{II}_{2,j,k}\sim 2^{2k+2j}L^{I}_{2,j,k}
) and the assumption
j+k\leq-\delta m/3\leq 0
.
∎

From now on we assume that
n\geq 10
. We first isolate the main contribution.

Lemma 5.7
.

For any
\star\in\{I,II\}
, we have

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}L^{\star}_{2,j,k}(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dsdv&=I_{n,j,k}^{\star}(x,t)+Rem_{n,j,k}^{\star}(x,t)\end{split}

where
Rem_{n,j,k}^{\star}
denote acceptable terms,

\begin{split}\text{ if }\,\,\,j+k\geq-\delta m/3\,\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\,\|\langle t\rangle Rem_{n,j,k}^{I}\|_{B_{t}^{0}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-(k+j)+\delta m/10},\\
\text{ if }\,\,\,j+k\leq-\delta m/3\,\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\,\|\langle t\rangle Rem_{n,j,k}^{II}\|_{B_{t}^{0}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{\delta m/10}.\end{split}

(5.35)

The main terms
I^{\star}_{n,j,k},\star\in\{I,II\}
, can be written as a linear superposition of terms of the form

\begin{split}&I^{\star}_{n,j,k}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{-5j-k-n}\iint_{\{0\leq s\leq\tau\leq t\}}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathfrak{I}^{\star}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[P_{k_{1}}\rho,P_{k_{2}}\rho]\,dvdsd\tau,\\
&\mathfrak{I}^{I}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[f,g](x,v,\tau,s,t)=\mathfrak{I}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[f,g](x,v,\tau,s,t),\\
&\mathfrak{I}^{II}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[f,g](x,v,\tau,s,t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}^{II}_{j,k}(x-y,v)\mathfrak{I}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[f,g](y,v,\tau,s,t)dy,\\
&\mathfrak{I}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[f,g](x,v,\tau,s,t)=\omega_{k_{1}}(\tau,s)\omega_{k_{2}}(\tau,s)\cdot b(v,s,t)\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times P_{[k-2,k+2]}\mathcal{R}^{1}\left[(\mathcal{R}^{2}f)(x-(t-s)v,s)\cdot(\mathcal{R}^{3}g)(x-(t-\tau)v,\tau)\right],\end{split}

(5.36)

where
\mathcal{R}^{a}
denote normalized Calderon-Zygmund operators (as defined in (
5.11
)) and

\begin{split}\omega_{k_{1}}(\tau,s)\in\{\tau-s,2^{-k_{1}}\},\qquad\omega_{k_{2}}(\tau,s)\in\{\tau-s,2^{-k_{2}}\},\qquad\|b\|_{L_{v,s,t}^{\infty}}+2^{n}\|\partial_{s}b\|_{L_{v,s,t}^{\infty}}\lesssim 1.\end{split}

(5.37)

Proof.

Recall (
5.30
). Notice that the only difference between
L^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)
and
L^{II}_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)
lies in the kernels, which play a minor role, so it would be sufficient to consider
L^{I}_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)
in detail here. We observe that for any function
F
,

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}F(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)dv&=\frac{1}{2^{k}(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left[(t-s)\partial_{x^{r}}+\partial_{v^{r}}\right]P^{r}_{k}F(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)\,dv\\
&=\frac{1}{2^{k}(t-s)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P^{r}_{k}(\partial_{v^{r}}F)(x-(t-s)v,v,s,t)\,dv,\\
\end{split}

where
P_{k}^{r}:=-2^{k}\partial_{x^{r}}|\nabla|^{-2}P_{k}
has the same properties as
P_{k}
. Using (
2.23
) we calculate

\begin{split}\partial_{v^{r}}&L_{2,j}(x,v,s,t)=\big\{E(x,s)-E(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\big\}\cdot\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\\
&\quad+E(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\cdot\big[\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)-\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))\big]\\
&\quad+\nu^{q}_{r}(x,v,s,t)\partial_{x^{q}}E(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))\\
&\quad+\mu_{r}^{q}(x,v,s,t)E(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\cdot\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t)),\end{split}

(5.38)

where

\begin{split}&\nu_{r}^{q}(x,v,s,t):=\big[(s\partial_{x^{r}}-\partial_{v^{r}})\widetilde{Y}^{q}\big](x-sv,v,s,t),\\
&\mu_{r}^{q}(x,v,s,t):=\big[\left(s\partial_{x^{r}}-\partial_{v^{r}}\right)\widetilde{W}^{q}\big](x-sv,v,s,t).\end{split}

As a result of direct computations using (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\nu^{q}_{r}(x,v,s,t)=-\int_{s}^{t}(\tau-s)^{2}\partial_{x^{r}}E^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\,d\tau

(5.39)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{s}^{t}(\tau-s)(s\partial_{x^{r}}-\partial_{v^{r}})\widetilde{Y}^{q^{\prime}}(x-sv,v,\tau,t)\partial_{x^{q^{\prime}}}E^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)d\tau,

(5.40)

\displaystyle\mu^{q}_{r}(x,v,s,t)=\int_{s}^{t}(\tau-s)\partial_{x^{r}}E^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\,d\tau

(5.41)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{s}^{t}(s\partial_{x^{r}}-\partial_{v^{r}})\widetilde{Y}^{q^{\prime}}(x-sv,v,\tau,t)\partial_{x^{q^{\prime}}}E^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)d\tau.

(5.42)

After localizing the frequencies of the two electric fields, we have

\begin{split}\partial_{v^{r}}L_{2,j,k}(x,v,s,t)&=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{s}^{t}P_{k}\left[\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}\right](x,v,\tau,s,t)d\tau,\end{split}

where

\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}=\mathcal{M}^{0}_{k_{1},k_{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{13}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{i},

(5.43)

and, with
E_{l}:=P_{l}E
,

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{0}(x,v,\tau,s,t)&:=-(\tau-s)\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\cdot\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\\
&+E_{k_{1}}(x,s)E_{k_{2}}^{p}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\cdot\partial_{v^{r}}\partial_{v^{p}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\\
&-(\tau-s)^{2}\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\cdot M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\\
&+(\tau-s)E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\cdot\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{1}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=-(\tau-s)\int_{0}^{1}\big[\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\theta\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)-\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\big]\\
&\qquad\times E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\cdot\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\,d\theta,\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{2}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=-(\tau-s)\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\\
&\qquad\times\big[E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)-E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\big]\cdot\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{3}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=\int_{0}^{1}\big[\partial_{v^{q}}\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\theta\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))-\partial_{v^{r}}\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big]\\
&\qquad\cdot E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\,d\theta,\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{4}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{v^{q}}\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\cdot\big[E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)-E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\big]\\
&\qquad\times E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\,d\theta,\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{5}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{v^{q}}\partial_{v^{r}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\cdot E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\\
&\qquad\times\big[E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)-E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\big]\,d\theta,\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{6}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=-(\tau-s)^{2}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))\cdot\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\\
&\qquad\times\big[\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)-\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\big],\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{7}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=-(\tau-s)^{2}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))\\
&\qquad\cdot\big[\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)-\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\big]\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{8}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=-(\tau-s)^{2}\big[M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))-M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big]\\
&\qquad\cdot\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{9}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=(\tau-s)\big[\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))-\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big]\\
&\qquad\cdot E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\partial_{x^{r}}E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{10}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=(\tau-s)\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\cdot\big[E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)-E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\big]\\
&\qquad\times\partial_{x^{r}}E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{11}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=(\tau-s)\big[\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\cdot E_{k_{1}}(x,s)\\
&\qquad\times\big[\partial_{x^{r}}E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)-\partial_{x^{r}}E_{k_{2}}^{q}(x-(s-\tau)v,\tau)\big],\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{12}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=(\tau-s)(s\partial_{x^{r}}-\partial_{v^{r}})\widetilde{Y}^{q^{\prime}}(x-sv,v,\tau,t)\partial_{x^{q}}E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\\
&\qquad\cdot\partial_{x^{q^{\prime}}}E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{13}(x,v,\tau,s,t):=-(s\partial_{x^{r}}-\partial_{v^{r}})\widetilde{Y}^{q^{\prime}}(x-sv,v,\tau,t)E_{k_{1}}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)\\
&\qquad\cdot\partial_{x^{q^{\prime}}}E^{q}_{k_{2}}(x-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\partial_{v^{q}}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t)).\end{split}

Direct inspection shows that all terms in
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{k_{1},k_{2}}
lead to terms of the form (
5.36
) as claimed, whereas for the error terms
\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{i}
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,13\}
, we can apply Lemma
5.9
to conclude.
∎

We focus now on the terms in (
5.36
) and prove the following lemma.

Lemma 5.8
.

With the hypothesis of Lemma
5.7
and the notations inside its proof, if
m\geq\delta^{-4}
,
j\in[0,19m/20]
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
n\in[10,m+2]
then

\begin{split}\text{ if }\,\,\,j+k\geq-\delta m/3\,\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\,\|\langle t\rangle I^{I}_{n,j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-(k+j)+\delta m/2},\\
\text{ if }\,\,\,j+k\leq-\delta m/3\,\,\,\text{ then }\,\,\,\|\langle t\rangle I^{II}_{n,j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{\delta m/2}.\end{split}

(5.44)

Proof.

We focus on the estimate of
I^{I}_{n,j,k}
. With minor modifications, the estimate of
I^{II}_{n,j,k}
can be obtained as a byproduct.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
k_{1}\leq k_{2}
and apply the decomposition (
2.36
) for the first input (in the case
k_{2}\leq k_{1}
we apply the decomposition in (
2.36
) for the second input, use the same strategy with the only difference that we do integration by parts in
\tau
instead of
s
for the oscillatory part, which gives us better estimates since
\tau\geq s
).

Note that
k\leq k_{2}+10.
Moreover, we use the following partition to localize the size of
\tau
,

\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}(\tau)=\sum_{0\leq\tilde{m}\leq m+2}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}(\tau).

(5.45)

For simplicity of notation, let
\rho_{l}:=P_{l}\rho
,
\rho_{l}^{stat}:=P_{l}(\rho^{stat})
,
\rho_{l}^{osc}:=P_{l}(\rho^{osc})
.

Step
1
: the static component.
We will show that

\begin{split}&\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z},\,k_{2}\geq k-10}\sum_{\tilde{m}\in[0,m+2]}2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big[\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[\rho_{k_{1}}^{stat},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+2^{3m}\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[\rho_{k_{1}}^{stat},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\big]\,d\tau ds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-m+\delta m/2}.\end{split}

(5.46)

A simple estimate using (
3.5
)-(
3.6
) and the fact that
k_{2}\geq k-10
shows that

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[\rho_{k_{1}}^{stat},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)2^{2\max\{-k_{1},\tilde{m}\}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\min\{\|\rho^{stat}_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}},\|\rho^{stat}_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j-k_{2}-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)2^{2\max\{-k_{1},\tilde{m}\}}2^{3\min\{k_{1},-\tilde{m}\}}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-1}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k_{2}}2^{\min\{k_{1},-\tilde{m}\}}2^{4\delta\tilde{m}}2^{n-2m},\end{split}

(5.47)

where the last inequality follows by considering the two possible cases
(n\leq m-10\text{ and }\widetilde{m}\geq m-10)
and
n\geq m-10
. One can sum over
k_{1},k_{2},\tilde{m}
and recall the assumption
j+k\geq-\delta m/3
to see that this gives an acceptable contribution to (
5.46
).

To bound
L^{\infty}
norms we use the general estimate

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|f(x-\lambda_{1}v)|&|g(x-\lambda_{2}v)|\varphi(2^{-j}v)\,dv\\
&\lesssim\min\big\{2^{3j}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}},|\lambda_{1}|^{-3}\|f\|_{L^{1}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}},|\lambda_{2}|^{-3}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{L^{1}}\big\},\end{split}

(5.48)

for any functions
f,g\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, and any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

Using this estimate we bound first the contribution of large times
s
,

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\int_{t/4}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)2^{3m}\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[\rho_{k_{1}}^{stat},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j-n+3m}\int_{t/4}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)(2^{-k_{1}}+\tau)^{2}\|\rho_{k_{1}}^{stat}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k_{2}}2^{\min(k_{1},-m)}2^{n-2m+4\delta m}.\end{split}

(5.49)

Similarly, letting
\overline{s}:=\max\{s,\min\{2^{-k_{1}},t/2\}\}
, we use again (
5.48
) and (
3.5
)-(
3.6
) to estimate

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{\overline{s}}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)2^{3m}\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[\rho^{stat}_{k_{1}},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j+2m}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{\overline{s}}^{t}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)(2^{-k_{1}}+\tau)^{2}\min(2^{3k_{1}},2^{-3m})\|\rho^{stat}_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{1}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k_{2}}2^{\min(k_{1},-\tilde{m})}2^{-m+4\delta\tilde{m}}.\end{split}

(5.50)

and

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{s}^{\overline{s}}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)2^{3m}\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[\rho^{stat}_{k_{1}},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-4j+2m}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{s}^{\overline{s}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)(2^{-k_{1}}+\tau)^{2}\|\rho^{stat}_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{-3m}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k_{2}}2^{\min(k_{1},-\tilde{m})}2^{-m+4\delta\tilde{m}}.\end{split}

(5.51)

The desired bounds (
5.46
) follow from (
5.47
) and (
5.49
), (
5.50
), (
5.51
).

Step 2: the oscillatory component.
We now consider the contribution of the oscillatory field. We estimate first as in (
5.47
), (
5.49
), (
5.50
), (
5.51
), with additional factors of
\min(2^{-k_{1}},\langle\tau\rangle)
coming from (
3.5
)–(
3.6
). The result is

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\widetilde{\varphi}_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big[\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-i\iota s}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc,\iota},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+2^{3m}\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-i\iota s}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc,\iota},\rho_{k_{2}}](\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\big]\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k_{2}}2^{\min\{k_{1},-\tilde{m}\}}2^{-m+4\delta\tilde{m}}2^{\min\{\tilde{m},-k_{1}\}},\end{split}

(5.52)

where
\iota\in\{-1,1\}
,
\rho_{l}^{osc,+}:=\rho_{l}^{osc}
,
\rho_{l}^{osc,-}:=\overline{\rho_{l}^{osc}}
.
This suffices to bound the contributions corresponding to either
|k_{1}+\tilde{m}|\geq 4\delta\tilde{m}
, or
k_{2}\geq k+4\delta\tilde{m}
, or
\tilde{m}\leq m/40
. After these reductions it remains to prove that for
q\in\{1,\infty\}
we have

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}2^{3m(1-1/q)}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-\iota is}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc,\iota},\rho_{k_{2}}](x,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvd\tau ds\Big\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim 2^{-m+2\delta m/5},\end{split}

(5.53)

provided that
m\geq\delta^{-4}
,
j\in[0,19m/20]
,
n\in[10,m+2]
,
j+k\geq-\delta m/3
, and

\tilde{m}\in[m/40,m+2],\qquad k_{1}\in[-\tilde{m}-4\delta\tilde{m},-\tilde{m}+4\delta\tilde{m}],\qquad k_{2}\in[k-10,k+4\delta\tilde{m}].

(5.54)

By taking complex conjugates, in proving (
5.53
) we may assume that
\iota=+1
. Let

\rho_{l,\leq p}^{osc}:=\Pi_{-1,l,\leq p}\rho^{osc},\qquad\rho_{l,>p}^{osc}:=\Pi_{-1,l,>p}\rho^{osc},\qquad\Gamma_{l,>p}:=\Pi_{-1,l,>p}\omega^{-1}_{-1,0}\rho^{osc},

(5.55)

for
l\in\mathbb{Z}
and
p\leq-4
, where the operators
\Pi_{\pm 1,l,\leq p}
,
\Pi_{\pm 1,l,>p}
,
\omega^{-1}_{\pm 1,0}
are defined in (
3.15
)–(
3.16
).

To bound the contribution of
\rho_{k_{1},>p_{0}}^{osc}
we would like to integrate by parts in
s
(the method of normal forms). We start from the identity

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-is}\rho_{k_{1},>p_{0}}^{osc},\rho_{k_{2}}](x,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvds\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\omega_{k_{1}}(\tau,s)\omega_{k_{2}}(\tau,s)b(v,s,t)\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)A_{k}(x-y)\\
&\qquad\times e^{-is(1-v\cdot\xi)}\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc}}(\xi,s)\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-v\cdot\xi)e^{i(y-tv)\cdot\xi}(\mathcal{R}^{3}\rho_{k_{2}})(y-(t-\tau)v,\tau)\,dsd\xi dvdy,\end{split}

(5.56)

where the kernel
A_{k}
is defined by
\widehat{A_{k}}(\eta)=\varphi_{[k-2,k+2]}(\eta)\mathcal{R}^{1}(\eta)
. Then we use the identity

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\tau}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\omega_{k_{1}}(\tau,s)\omega_{k_{2}}(\tau,s)b(v,s,t)e^{-is(1-v\cdot\xi)}\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc}}(\xi,s)\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-v\cdot\xi)\,ds\\
&=\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{e^{-is(1-v\cdot\xi)}\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-v\cdot\xi)}{i(1-v\cdot\xi)}\big[B(v,\tau,s,t)(\partial_{s}\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc}})(\xi,s)+(\partial_{s}B)(v,\tau,s,t)\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc}}(\xi,s)\big]\\
&+\frac{\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-v\cdot\xi)}{i(1-v\cdot\xi)}B(v,\tau,0,t)\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc}}(\xi,0)-\frac{e^{-i\tau(1-v\cdot\xi)}\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-v\cdot\xi)}{i(1-v\cdot\xi)}B(v,\tau,\tau,t)\widehat{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{1}}^{osc}}(\xi,\tau),\end{split}

(5.57)

where

B(v,\tau,s,t)=B_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(v,\tau,s,t):=\varphi_{n}(t-s)\omega_{k_{1}}(\tau,s)\omega_{k_{2}}(\tau,s)b(v,s,t).

(5.58)

We combine the formulas (
5.56
)–(
5.57
) and examine the
\xi
-integral. Notice that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}&\frac{e^{isv\cdot\xi}\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-v\cdot\xi)}{i(1-v\cdot\xi)}e^{i(y-tv)\cdot\xi}\widehat{F_{k_{1}}}(\xi,s)\,d\xi=-i\big(\Pi_{-1,k_{1},>p_{0}}\omega^{-1}_{-1,0}F\big)(y-(t-s)v,v,s),\end{split}

according to the definitions (
3.15
)–(
3.16
), where
F\in\{\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho^{osc},\partial_{s}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho^{osc}\}
. Thus

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-is}\rho_{k_{1},>p_{0}}^{osc},\rho_{k_{2}}](x,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvds=\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(-i)e^{-is}\\
&\qquad\times A_{k}(x-y)\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)\cdot(\mathcal{R}^{3}\rho_{k_{2}})(y-(t-\tau)v,\tau)X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvdyds,\end{split}

(5.59)

where the functions
\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}}
are defined as in (
5.55
), and

\begin{split}X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t):&=B_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(v,\tau,s,t)(\mathcal{R}^{2}\partial_{s}\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}})(y-(t-s)v,v,s)\\
&+B^{\prime}_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(v,\tau,s,t)(\mathcal{R}^{2}\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}})(y-(t-s)v,v,s),\\
B^{\prime}_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(v,\tau,s,t):&=(\partial_{s}B_{n,k_{1},k_{2}})(v,\tau,s,t)+B_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(v,\tau,s,t)[\delta_{0}(s)-\delta_{0}(s-\tau)].\end{split}

(5.60)

Case 1: the
L^{1}
bounds.
We prove first the bounds (
5.53
) when
q=1
. Let
p_{0}:=-100\delta\tilde{m}
. We use Lemma
3.3
(i) (so
\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc}\equiv 0
unless
j+k_{1}\geq-4
) and estimate as in (
5.47
),

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-is}\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc},\rho_{k_{2}}]\big\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)(2^{-k_{1}}+\tau)^{2}\|\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-n-k_{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)(2^{-2k_{1}}+2^{2\tilde{m}})2^{3k_{1}+p_{0}}2^{-k_{1}}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-1}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\,\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k-m},\end{split}

(5.61)

using also the assumptions (
5.54
).

To bound the contribution of
\rho_{k_{1},>p_{0}}^{osc}
we use the formulas (
5.59
)–(
5.60
). In view of (
3.6
)–(
3.7
) and Lemma
3.3
(ii) we estimate

\big\|\mathcal{R}^{2}\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}}(s)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}L^{1}_{y}}+\langle s\rangle\big\|\mathcal{R}^{2}\partial_{s}\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}}(s)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}L^{1}_{y}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-p_{0}}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta}.

(5.62)

Therefore, using also the assumptions (
5.54
) we estimate

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\tau}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)&\cdot(\mathcal{R}^{3}\rho_{k_{2}})(y-(t-\tau)v,\tau)X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{1}_{y,v}}\,ds\\
&\lesssim\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}2^{3j}\|X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}L^{1}_{s}L^{1}_{y}}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{3j}2^{-k_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-4+2\delta}\cdot 2^{-p_{0}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2+12\delta}.\end{split}

Therefore, using the formula (
5.59
) and the definition (
5.58
) we estimate

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-is}\rho_{k_{1},>p_{0}}^{osc},\rho_{k_{2}}](x,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvd\tau ds\Big\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim 2^{-4j-n}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\varphi_{\leq n+2}(t-\tau)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\int_{0}^{\tau}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)(\mathcal{R}^{3}\rho_{k_{2}})(y-(t-\tau)v,\tau)X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t)\big\|_{L^{1}_{y,v}}dsd\tau\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k_{2}-n}2^{-p_{0}}2^{-2\tilde{m}+20\delta\tilde{m}}\min\{2^{\tilde{m}},2^{n}\}.\end{split}

(5.63)

The desired bounds (
5.53
) follow in the case
q=1
, using also (
5.61
).

Case 2: the
L^{\infty}
bounds.
We prove now the bounds (
5.53
) when
q=\infty
. As before we let
p_{0}=-100\delta\tilde{m}
, and recall the definition (
5.55
).

We first bound the contribution of the function
\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc}
, which is nontrivial only if
j+k_{1}\geq-4
. For
s\in[0,t]
let
s^{\prime}:=\max\{s,t/4\}
. Using Lemma
3.5
(i) and the assumptions (
5.54
) (in particular the integrals below are nontrivial only if
\tilde{m}\geq m-10
and
|k_{1}+m|\leq 4\delta m
),

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}2^{3m}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-is}\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc},\rho_{k_{2}}]\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j-n}2^{3m}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big[2^{-k_{1}}+\tau\big]^{2}\|\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-j-n-k_{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big[2^{-k_{1}}+\tau\big]^{2}2^{3k_{1}+p_{0}}2^{-k_{1}}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-1}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\,\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k-m-10\delta\tilde{m}}.\end{split}

(5.64)

This suffices to prove the desired bounds if
n\leq m-4
. In the remaining cases we may assume that
n\geq m-4
and
s\leq t/4
, and we can estimate the remaining integral using (
5.48
),

\begin{split}&2^{-4j-n}2^{3m}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{s}^{t/4}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big\|\mathfrak{J}_{kk_{1}k_{2}}[e^{-is}\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc},\rho_{k_{2}}]\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{1}_{v}}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-4j-m}2^{3m}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{s}^{t/4}\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big[2^{-k_{1}}+\tau\big]^{2}\|\rho_{k_{1},\leq p_{0}}^{osc}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}L^{\infty}_{x}}2^{-3m}\|\rho_{k_{2}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim 2^{-4j-m-k_{2}}\int_{0}^{t/4}\int_{s}^{t/4}\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\big[2^{-k_{1}}+\tau\big]^{2}2^{3k_{1}+p_{0}}2^{-k_{1}}\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-1}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\,\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-k-m-10\delta\tilde{m}}.\end{split}

(5.65)

Finally, we consider the contribution from the oscillatory part
\rho_{k_{1},>p_{0}}^{osc}
. As in the
L^{1}
-estimate, we integrate by parts in
s
once. In view of the formula (
5.59
), for (
5.53
) it suffices to prove that

\begin{split}2^{-4j-n}&2^{3m}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)\varphi_{[n-2,n+2]}(t-s)\big|\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)(\mathcal{R}^{3}\rho_{k_{2}})(y-(t-\tau)v,\tau)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t)\big|\,dvdsd\tau\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\lesssim 2^{-m+2\delta m/5}.\end{split}

(5.66)

We consider two cases. If
n\leq m-6
then we may assume that
\widetilde{m}\geq m-6
and
t/2\leq s\leq\tau\leq t
. We examine the identities (
5.58
) and (
5.60
), and use Lemma
3.5
(ii) to estimate

\begin{split}\|X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,&t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,v}}\lesssim(2^{-k_{1}}+2^{m})^{2}2^{-p_{0}}\\
&\big[\|\partial_{s}\rho^{osc}_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+2^{-n}\|\rho^{osc}_{k_{1}}(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\rho^{osc}_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\delta_{0}(s-\tau)\big].\end{split}

Thus, using (
3.5
)–(
3.7
) and (
5.54
), the left-hand side of (
5.66
) is bounded by

\begin{split}C\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-n}&2^{3m}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{\leq n+2}(t-s)2^{-k_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-4+2\delta}2^{-p_{0}}(2^{-k_{1}}+2^{m})^{3}\langle s\rangle^{-4+2\delta}(2^{-n}+\delta_{0}(s-\tau))d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j-n-k}2^{-1.9m},\end{split}

which clearly gives the desired bounds (
5.66
) in this case.

On the other hand, if
n\geq m-6
then we may assume that
t-s\geq 2^{m-6}
. We use first the dispersive estimates (
3.34
), so

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{\leq j+4}(v)&\big[\langle s\rangle\big|(\mathcal{R}^{2}\partial_{s}\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}})(y-(t-s)v,v,s)\big|\\
&+\big|(\mathcal{R}^{2}\Gamma_{k_{1},>p_{0}})(y-(t-s)v,v,s)\big|\big]\,dv\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-p_{0}}2^{-3m}\langle s\rangle^{\delta}.\end{split}

Therefore, using the identities (
5.58
) and (
5.60
), if
s\leq\tau\leq 2^{\tilde{m}+4}
,

\|X_{n,k_{1},k_{2}}(y,v,\tau,s,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}L^{1}_{v}}\lesssim(2^{-k_{1}}+2^{\tilde{m}})^{2}\varepsilon_{1}2^{-p_{0}}2^{-3m}\big[\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}+\delta_{0}(s)+\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta}\delta_{0}(s-\tau)\big].

Thus, using (
3.5
)–(
3.6
) and (
5.54
), the left-hand side of (
5.66
) is bounded by

\begin{split}C\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-4j-m}&2^{3m}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\tau}\varphi_{\tilde{m}}(\tau)2^{-k_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-4+2\delta}\\
&\times(2^{-k_{1}}+2^{\tilde{m}})^{2}2^{-p_{0}}2^{-3m}\big[\langle s\rangle^{2\delta-1}+\delta_{0}(s)+\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta}\delta_{0}(s-\tau)\big]\,dsd\tau\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-4j-k}2^{-m-\tilde{m}/2},\end{split}

which clearly gives the desired bounds (
5.66
) in this case.
∎

For the error type terms
\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{l}
appearing in (
5.43
) we have:

Lemma 5.9
.

With the hypothesis of Lemma
5.7
and the notations inside its proof, if
m\geq\delta^{-4}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
j\in[0,19m/20]
,
n\in[10,m+2]
,
q\in\{1,\infty\}
, and
l\in\{1,\ldots,13\}
then

\begin{split}&2^{j-n}2^{3m(1-1/q)}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\varphi_{n}(t-s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{l}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvd\tau ds\Big\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-m+\delta m/10}.\end{split}

(5.67)

Proof.

Notice that the bounds (
5.67
) suffice to prove both estimates in (
5.35
): the estimates in the first line follow in the case
k+j+\delta m/3\geq 0
, while the estimates in the second line follow in the case
k+j+\delta m/3\geq 0
, using also the bounds (
5.31
).

Recall the definitions of the functions
\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{l}
following (
5.43
). Notice that factors
E_{k_{1}}
and
E_{k_{2}}
appear with either 0 or 1
x
-derivatives, so the contributions decay sufficiently fast when one of the indices
k_{1}
or
k_{2}
is either too small, or too large. It suffices to estimate the core contributions, coming from
k_{1},k_{2}\in[-10m,10m]
.

Most of the terms can be estimated using Lemma
5.1
. Indeed, with the notation (
5.1
)–(
5.2
),

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{2}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{2})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{2}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,\tau,t)\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{8}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{8})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{8}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)^{2}\big[M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t))-M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big],\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{10}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{10})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{10}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t)\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{12}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{12})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{12}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)(s\nabla_{y}-\nabla_{v})\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,\tau,t)M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)),\end{split}

where the notation
\sim
means that the function in the left-hand side can be written as a linear combination of functions in the right-hand side (various partial derivatives, various components, and in some cases, integrals from
0
to
1
). Using (
3.52
)–(
3.54
), we have
\Lambda(C^{l})(\tau,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-2+3\delta}
for
l\in\{2,8,10,12\}
. Therefore, using (
5.8
),

2^{3m(1-1/q)}\Big\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{l}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvd\tau\Big\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-2}2^{-j}

(5.68)

for any
s\in[0,t]
,
q\in\{1,\infty\}
, and
l\in\{2,8,10,12\}
. The desired bounds (
5.67
) follow by integration in
s
and summation over
k_{1},k_{2}\in[-10m,10m]
.

Similarly,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{4}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E_{k_{1}},E_{k_{2}};C^{4})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{4}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t)\nabla^{2}_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{5}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{5})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{5}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,\tau,t)\nabla^{2}_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{9}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{9})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{9}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)\big[\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t))-\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big],\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{13}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{13})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{13}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(s\nabla_{y}-\nabla_{v})\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,\tau,t)\nabla M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)).\end{split}

Using (
3.52
)–(
3.54
) we have
\Lambda(C^{l})(\tau,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle\langle s\rangle^{-2+3\delta}
for
l\in\{4,5,9,13\}
. Thus

2^{3m(1-1/q)}\Big\|\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{l}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvd\tau\Big\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-2}2^{-j},

using (
5.5
)–(
5.6
), for any
s\in[0,t]
,
q\in\{1,\infty\}
, and
l\in\{4,5,9,13\}
. The desired bounds (
5.67
) follow by integration in
s
and summation over
k_{1},k_{2}\in[-10m,10m]
, as before.

Similarly,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{3}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E_{k_{1}},E_{k_{2}};C^{3})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{3}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}\big[\nabla_{v}^{2}M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\theta\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t))-\nabla_{v}^{2}M^{\prime}_{j}(v)\big],\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{1}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla^{2}E_{k_{1}},E_{k_{2}};C^{1})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{1}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t)\nabla M^{\prime}_{j}(v),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{7}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla^{2}E_{k_{1}},\nabla E_{k_{2}};C^{7})(x,\tau,s,t),\\
&\qquad C^{7}(x,y,v,\tau,s,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)^{2}\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t)M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)).\end{split}

Clearly,
\Lambda(C^{3})(\tau,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-2+3\delta}
,
\Lambda(C^{1})(\tau,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle\langle s\rangle^{-1+3\delta}
, and
\Lambda(C^{7})(\tau,s,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-1+3\delta}
, using (
3.52
). Bounds similar to (
5.68
) follow for
l\in\{3,1,7\}
.

In the remaining cases
l=6
and
l=11
we need to reverse the roles of the variables
\tau
and
s
. Indeed,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{6}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla^{2}E_{k_{2}},\nabla E_{k_{1}};C^{6})(x,s,\tau,t),\\
&\qquad C^{6}(x,y,v,s,\tau,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)^{2}\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,\tau,t)M^{\prime}_{j}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)),\end{split}

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{11}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dv\sim\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla^{2}E_{k_{2}},E_{k_{2}};C^{11})(x,s,\tau,t),\\
&\qquad C^{11}(x,y,v,s,\tau,t):=2^{3j}(\tau-s)\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,\tau,t)\nabla_{v}M^{\prime}_{j}(v).\end{split}

Using (
3.52
) we have
\Lambda(C^{6})(s,\tau,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle^{1+2\delta}
and
\Lambda(C^{11})(s,\tau,t)\lesssim 2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta}
. Thus

2^{3m(1-1/q)}\Big\|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}P_{k}\mathcal{M}_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{l}(x-(t-s)v,v,\tau,s,t)\,dvds\Big\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle\tau\rangle^{-2}2^{-j},

using (
5.9
)–(
5.10
), for any
\tau\in[0,t]
,
q\in\{1,\infty\}
, and
l\in\{6,11\}
. The desired bounds (
5.67
) follow by integration in
\tau
and summation over
k_{1},k_{2}\in[-10m,10m]
.
∎

6.
Bounds on the type-II reaction term

In this section we prove the following:

Proposition 6.1
.

With the notation in (
2.44
), under the assumptions (
2.41
) of Proposition
2.4
we have

\|\rho^{osc}_{2,II}\|_{Osc_{\delta}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}.

(6.1)

Proof.

In view of the definitions, for (
6.1
) it suffices to prove that, for any
k,m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
m\geq\delta^{-4}
,

\Big\|\sum_{j\in[0,19m/20]\cap[0,-k-\delta m/3]}\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)T^{II}_{2,j,k}\Big\|_{Osc_{\delta}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}.

(6.2)

We recall (
2.34
), and start from the definition (
2.23
) to calculate
(\partial_{t}L_{2,j})(x,v,s,t)
. For this we notice that by definition (
3.1
) we have

\begin{split}&X(x+(t-s)v,v,s,t)=\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t)+x,\\
&V(x+(t-s)v,v,s,t)=\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t)+v.\end{split}

(6.3)

Therefore

\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}L_{2,j})(x,v,s,t)=\widetilde{\varphi}_{j}(v)\\
&\times[-(\partial_{i}E^{l})(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)(\partial_{t}\widetilde{Y}^{i})(x-sv,v,s,t)\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))\\
&\quad\,\,-E^{l}(x+\widetilde{Y}(x-sv,v,s,t),s)(\partial_{t}\widetilde{W}^{i})(x-sv,v,s,t)\partial_{i}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-sv,v,s,t))\big].\end{split}

Using the formulas (
3.2
) we have

\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}\widetilde{Y})(x-sv,v,s,t)=(t-s)E(x+(t-s)v,t)+Err^{1}(x+(t-s)v,v,s,t),\\
&Err^{1}_{i}(x,v,s,t)\!:=\!\int_{s}^{t}(\tau-s)(\nabla E_{i})(x-tv+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,\tau,t)d\tau,\end{split}

(6.4)

and

\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}\widetilde{W})(x-sv,v,s,t)=-E(x+(t-s)v,t)-Err^{2}(x+(t-s)v,v,s,t),\\
&Err^{2}_{i}(x,v,s,t)\!:=\!\int_{s}^{t}(\nabla E_{i})(x-tv+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,\tau,t),\tau)\cdot\partial_{t}\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,\tau,t)d\tau.\end{split}

(6.5)

Therefore we can decompose

(\partial_{t}L_{2,j})(x,v,s,t)=\big[N_{1}(x+(t-s)v,v,s,t)+N_{2}(x+(t-s)v,v,s,t)\big]\widetilde{\varphi}_{j}(v),

(6.6)

where

\begin{split}N_{1}&(y,v,s,t):=-(\partial_{i}E^{l})(y-(t-s)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t),s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot(t-s)E^{i}(y,t)\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t))\\
&+E^{l}(y-(t-s)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t),s)\cdot E^{i}(y,t)\partial_{i}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)),\end{split}

(6.7)

and

\displaystyle N_{2}(y,v,s,t)

\displaystyle=N^{1}_{2}(y,v,s,t)+N^{2}_{2}(y,v,s,t),

(6.8)

\displaystyle N^{1}_{2}(y,v,s,t)

\displaystyle:=-(\partial_{i}E^{l})(y-(t-s)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t),s)\cdot Err^{1}_{i}(y,v,s,t)

(6.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)),

(6.10)

\displaystyle N^{2}_{2}(y,v,s,t)

\displaystyle:=E^{l}(y-(t-s)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-tv,v,s,t),s)\cdot Err^{2}_{i}(y,v,s,t)

(6.11)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\partial_{i}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-tv,v,s,t)).

(6.12)

We can use the formulas (
6.6
)–(
6.9
) and (
2.34
) to decompose

\displaystyle T^{II}_{2,j,k}

\displaystyle=O^{1}_{j,k}+O^{2}_{j,k},

(6.13)

\displaystyle\widehat{O^{a}_{j,k}}(\xi,t)

\displaystyle:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{+}(s-\tau)e^{-(t-s)|\xi|}\frac{e^{is}}{1-i\mathcal{D}}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\widetilde{\varphi}_{j}(v)\widehat{N_{a}}(\xi,v,\tau,s)\,dvd\tau ds

for
a\in\{1,2\}
, where as before
\mathcal{D}=|\xi|-iv\cdot\xi
. For future reference we denote the spatial kernel of this expression by

\mathcal{L}_{j,k}(z,v,s,t):=\widetilde{\varphi}_{j}(v)\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz\cdot\xi}e^{-(t-s)|\xi|}\frac{e^{is}}{1-i\mathcal{D}}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\,d\xi.

(6.14)

Notice that for
(j,k,m)\in A^{II}
we have
k+j\leq-10
, so we can integrate by parts several times in
\xi
in the definition above to see that

|\mathcal{L}_{j,k}(z,v,s,t)|\lesssim\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{3k}(1+2^{k}|z|)^{-10}.

(6.15)

Lemmas
6.2
–
6.4
below, together with the further decompositions (
6.28
) and (
6.30
), yield the desired bounds (
6.2
).
∎

We estimate first the remainder terms
O^{2}_{j,k}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
6.1
, for any
(j,k,m)\in A^{II}
we have

\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t){O}^{2}_{j,k}\|_{Osc_{\delta}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-\delta j}.

(6.16)

Proof.

We decompose

\begin{split}&{O}^{2}_{j,k}={O}^{2,1}_{j,k}+{O}^{2,2}_{j,k},\\
&{O}^{2,l}_{j,k}(x,t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{+}(s-\tau)\mathcal{L}_{j,k}(x-y,v,s,t)N^{l}_{2}(y,v,\tau,s)\,dydvd\tau ds.\end{split}

(6.17)

Step 1.
We consider first the case
l=1
, and examine the formula for
N^{1}_{2}
in (
6.9
) and the formula for the functions
Err^{1}
in (
6.4
). We have

\begin{split}{O}^{2,1}_{j,k}(x,t):=\int_{0}^{t}&\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}_{j,k}(x-y,v,s,t)(-\partial_{i}E^{l})(y-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s),\tau)\\
&\times(\gamma-\tau)(\partial_{a}E^{i})(y-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s),\gamma)\\
&\times(\partial_{s}\widetilde{Y}^{a})(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\,dydvd\gamma d\tau ds.\end{split}

(6.18)

Notice that, with the notation in (
5.1
), for a suitable kernel
\mathcal{K}_{j,k}
satisfying (
5.2
) we have

\begin{split}&{O}^{2,1}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{i}E^{l},\partial_{a}E^{i};C_{al}^{1})(x,\gamma,\tau,s;t)\,d\gamma d\tau ds,\\
&C^{1}_{al}(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s;t):=(\gamma-\tau)(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(\partial_{s}\widetilde{Y}^{a})(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times 2^{3j}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-4,j+4]}(v).\end{split}

(6.19)

Clearly, using (
3.48
)–(
3.49
) and Lemma
3.1
(ii) we have

|(\partial_{s}\widetilde{Y}^{a})(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)|+\langle\gamma\rangle|(\partial_{\gamma}\partial_{s}\widetilde{Y}^{a})(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)|\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}.

(6.20)

Since
\|\partial_{l}M_{0}(v)\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-4,j+4]}(v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{-5j}
, with the notation in (
5.3
) we have

\Lambda(C^{1}_{al})(\gamma,\tau,s)\lesssim\langle\gamma\rangle(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}2^{-2j}.

(6.21)

We decompose
{O}^{2,1}_{j,k}={O}^{2,1,1}_{j,k}+{O}^{2,1,2}_{j,k}
where

\begin{split}&{O}^{2,1,1}_{j,k}(x,t):=\int_{0}^{t/2}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{i}E^{l},\partial_{a}E^{i};C_{al}^{1})(x,\gamma,\tau,s;t)\,d\gamma d\tau ds,\\
&{O}^{2,1,2}_{j,k}(x,t):=\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{i}E^{l},\partial_{a}E^{i};C_{al}^{1})(x,\gamma,\tau,s;t)\,d\gamma d\tau ds.\end{split}

Using now (
5.8
) and recalling that
k\leq 0
it follows that

\begin{split}\|{O}^{2,1,1}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&\lesssim\int_{0}^{t/2}\int_{0}^{s}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}2^{-2j}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}(1+2^{k}\langle t\rangle)^{-8}.\end{split}

(6.22)

Therefore, using Sobolev embedding,

\langle t\rangle^{3}\|{O}^{2,1,1}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}2^{3k}\langle t\rangle^{3}\langle t\rangle^{\delta}(1+2^{k}\langle t\rangle)^{-8}.

(6.23)

Moreover, using again (
5.8
), we can also estimate

\begin{split}\|{O}^{2,1,2}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&+\langle t\rangle^{3}\|{O}^{2,1,2}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}2^{-2j}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}\min\{2^{-k},\langle t\rangle\}.\end{split}

(6.24)

These three bounds show that

\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}{O}^{2,1}_{j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}+\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\langle t\rangle^{1-2\delta}\nabla_{x}{O}^{2,1}_{j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}.

(6.25)

We show now that

\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\langle t\rangle^{1-2\delta}(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{2,1}_{j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}.

(6.26)

Indeed, the identities (
6.18
) show that

(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{2,1}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{i}E^{l},\partial_{a}E^{i};C_{al}^{1})(x,\gamma,\tau,t;t)\,d\gamma d\tau.

It follows from (
5.8
) and (
6.21
) that

\|(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{2,1}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}2^{-2j}{\langle}\tau{\rangle}^{-1.1}\,d\tau\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}2^{-2j}.

The desired bounds (
6.26
) follow, which completes the analysis of the term
O^{2,1}_{j,k}
.

Step 2.
We consider now the case
l=2
, and examine the formula for
N^{2}_{2}
in (
6.9
) and the formula for the functions
Err^{2}
in (
6.5
). As before, with the notation in (
5.1
) and using (
6.15
), for a suitable kernel
\mathcal{K}_{j,k}
satisfying (
5.2
) we have

\begin{split}&{O}^{2,2}_{j,k}(x,t):=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E^{l},\partial_{a}E^{i};C_{ali}^{2})(x,\gamma,\tau,s;t)\,d\gamma d\tau ds,\\
&C^{2}_{ali}(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s;t):=(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(\partial_{s}\widetilde{Y}^{a})(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times 2^{3j}(\partial_{i}\partial_{l}M_{0})(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-4,j+4]}(v).\end{split}

(6.27)

Similarly to (
6.21
) we have

\Lambda(C^{2}_{ali})(\gamma,\tau,s)\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}2^{-2j}.

Then we estimate, using (
5.6
) and Sobolev embedding

\|{O}^{2,2}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}+\langle t\rangle^{3}\|{O}^{2,2}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}\min\{2^{-k},\langle t\rangle\}

as in (
6.22
), (
6.23
), and (
6.24
). Then we notice that

(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{2,2}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E^{l},\partial_{a}E^{i},C_{ali}^{2})(x,\gamma,\tau,t;t)\,d\gamma d\tau,

so we can estimate using (
5.6
)

\|(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{2,2}_{j,k}(.,t)\|_{B_{t}^{0}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}2^{-2j}{\langle}\tau{\rangle}^{-1.1}\,d\tau\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle t\rangle^{-1+\delta}2^{-2j}.

These bounds show that

\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\langle t\rangle^{-\delta}{O}^{2,2}_{j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}+\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\langle t\rangle^{1-2\delta}\nabla_{x}{O}^{2,2}_{j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}+\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\langle t\rangle^{1-2\delta}\partial_{t}{O}^{2,2}_{j,k}\|_{B^{0}_{t}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-2j}.

The lemma follows using also (
6.25
)–(
6.26
).
∎

We estimate now the main terms
{O}^{1}_{j,k}
defined in (
6.13
). Using the identities (
6.7
), we rewrite

\begin{split}N_{1}&=N_{1}^{1}+N_{1}^{2}+N_{1}^{3},\\
N_{1}^{1}(y,v,\tau,s)&:=\frac{d}{dv^{i}}\Big\{E^{l}(y-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s),\tau)E^{i}(y,s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\Big\},\\
N_{1}^{2}(y,v,\tau,s)&:=-(\partial_{a}E^{l})(y-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s),\tau)\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}_{i,a}(y,v,\tau,s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times E^{i}(y,s)\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)),\\
N_{1}^{3}(y,v,\tau,s)&:=-E^{l}(y-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s),\tau)E^{i}(y,s)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\widetilde{W}^{\prime}_{i,a}(y,v,\tau,s)(\partial_{a}\partial_{l}M_{0})(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)),\end{split}

(6.28)

where

\begin{split}\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}_{i,a}(y,v,\tau,s)&:=[(\partial_{v^{i}}-s\partial_{x^{i}})\widetilde{Y}^{a}](y-sv,v,\tau,s),\\
\widetilde{W}^{\prime}_{i,a}(y,v,\tau,s)&:=[(\partial_{v^{i}}-s\partial_{x^{i}})\widetilde{W}^{a}](y-sv,v,\tau,s).\end{split}

(6.29)

From the above decomposition, with the notation (
6.14
), we have

\begin{split}{O}^{1}_{j,k}(x,t)&=\sum_{l=1,2,3}O^{1,l}_{j,k}(x,t),\\
{O}^{1,l}_{j,k}(x,t)&:=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{+}(s-\tau)\mathcal{L}_{j,k}(x-y,v,s,t)N^{l}_{1}(y,v,\tau,s)\,dydvd\tau ds.\end{split}

(6.30)

We estimate first the main term:

Lemma 6.3
.

For any fixed
k,m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
m\geq\delta^{-4}
, we have

\Big\|\sum_{j\in[0,19m/20]\cap[0,-k-\delta m/3]}\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t){O}^{1,1}_{j,k}\Big\|_{Osc_{\delta}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}.

(6.31)

Proof.

Recall (
6.30
) and (
6.28
). After integrating by parts in
v
, we have

\begin{split}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}&(x,t)=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial_{v^{i}}\mathcal{L}_{j,k})(x-y,v,s,t)E^{i}(y,s)\\
&\times E^{l}(y-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s),\tau)\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\,dydvd\tau ds.\end{split}

(6.32)

Let
J_{0}:=\min\{19m/20,-k-\delta m/3\}
and consider the sum over
j
of these terms. We write

\begin{split}&\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}\partial_{v^{i}}\mathcal{L}_{j,k}(z,v,s,t)=\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}+2]}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{j,k}(z,v,s,t),\\
&\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{j,k}(z,v,s,t):=\widetilde{\varphi}_{j}(v)\frac{d}{dv^{i}}\Big\{\frac{\widetilde{\varphi}_{\leq J_{0}}(v)}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{iz\cdot\xi}e^{-(t-s)|\xi|}\frac{e^{is}}{1-i\mathcal{D}}\varphi_{k}(\xi)\,d\xi\Big\}.\end{split}

(6.33)

We integrate by parts a few times in
\xi
using Lemma
3.2
(notice that
J_{0}+k\leq-10
, so the denominator
1-i\mathcal{D}
in the integral is not singular) to see that

\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{j,k}(z,v,s,t)\big|\lesssim\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v)(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{3k}(1+2^{k}|z|)^{-10}\cdot(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j)).

(6.34)

We decompose
\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)O^{1,1}_{j,k}
via (
6.33
). Let

\begin{split}X_{j,k}&(x,s;t):=\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{j,k}(x-y,v,s,t)E^{i}(y,s)\\
&\times E^{l}(y-(s-\tau)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\tau,s),\tau)\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\,dydvd\tau,\end{split}

(6.35)

and notice that, due to (
6.32
),

\begin{split}&\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(x,t)=\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}+2]}\int_{0}^{t}X_{j,k}(x,s;t)\,ds,\\
&\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}\nabla_{x}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(x,t)=\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}+2]}\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{x}X_{j,k}(x,s;t)\,ds,\\
&\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(x,t)=\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}+2]}X_{j,k}(x,t;t).\end{split}

(6.36)

We prove now that for
r\in\{1,\infty\}

\begin{split}\langle s\rangle^{3-3/r}\|X_{j,k}(.,s;t)\|_{L^{r}_{x}}&\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))2^{-j},\\
\langle s\rangle^{3-3/r}\|\nabla_{x}X_{j,k}(.,s;t)\|_{L^{r}_{x}}&\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta},\end{split}

(6.37)

for any
k,j,s,t
. Indeed, we use (
6.34
) to express the functions
X_{j,k}
this in terms of trilinear operators as defined in (
5.1
), so that

\begin{split}&X_{j,k}(x,s;t)=\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E,E;C^{1})(x,\tau,s,s;t)\,d\tau ds,\\
&\quad C^{1}(x,y,v,\tau,s,s;t):=(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times 2^{3j}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v).\end{split}

(6.38)

Moreover, if we distribute one
\nabla_{x}
derivative in (
6.35
) we can write

\begin{split}&\nabla_{x}X_{j,k}=X^{1}_{j,k}+X^{2}_{j,k},\\
&X^{1}_{j,k}(x,s;t):=\int_{0}^{s}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E,\nabla E;C^{1})(x,\tau,s,s;t)+\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\nabla E,E;C^{2})(x,\tau,s,s;t)\big]\,d\tau,\\
&X^{2}_{j,k}(x,s;t):=\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(E,E;C^{3})(x,\tau,s,s;t)\,d\tau,\end{split}

(6.39)

where

\begin{split}&C^{2}(x,y,v,\tau,s,s;t):=(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))\\
&\qquad\qquad\times[\delta_{a}^{b}+\partial_{y^{a}}\widetilde{Y}^{b}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)]2^{3j}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v).\\
&C^{3}(x,y,v,\tau,s,s;t):=(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))\\
&\qquad\qquad\times 2^{3j}\partial_{y^{a}}\widetilde{W}^{b}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)\partial_{b}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s))\widetilde{\varphi}_{[j-2,j+2]}(v).\end{split}

(6.40)

The formulas (
6.38
) and (
6.40
) and the bounds (
3.53
) show that

\begin{split}\Lambda(C^{1})(\tau,s,s)+\Lambda(C^{2})(\tau,s,s)&\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))2^{-j},\\
\Lambda(C^{3})(\tau,s,s)&\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))2^{-j}\langle\tau\rangle^{-2}.\end{split}

(6.41)

The bounds in the first line of (
6.37
) follow from (
5.7
). The bounds in the second line follow from (
5.5
)–(
5.6
) for the term
X^{j,k}
and from (
5.12
) (by summation over
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
) for
X^{2}_{j,k}
.

We can now prove the bounds (
6.31
). Notice that

\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}+2]}(2^{k}+2^{-J_{0}}\mathfrak{1}_{[J_{0}-2,J_{0}+2]}(j))2^{-j}\lesssim 2^{k}+2^{-2J_{0}}\lesssim 2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2}.

(6.42)

We can thus use the formulas (
6.36
) and the bounds (
6.37
) to estimate

\begin{split}\Big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(t)\Big\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2})\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{\delta m/3},\end{split}

\begin{split}\Big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}\nabla_{x}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(t)\Big\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2})\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\,ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-m+4\delta m/3}.\end{split}

Also, using the Sobolev embedding when
s\leq t/2
, we estimate

\begin{split}\langle t\rangle^{3}\Big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)&\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(t)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{t/2}^{t}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2})\,ds\\
&+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t/2}\langle t\rangle^{3}2^{3k}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2})\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{\delta m/3}.\end{split}

\begin{split}\langle t\rangle^{3}\Big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)&\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}\nabla_{x}{O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(t)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{t/2}^{t}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2})\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\,ds\\
&+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t/2}\langle t\rangle^{3}2^{3k}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(2^{k+\delta m/3}+2^{-3m/2})\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\,ds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-m+4\delta m/3}.\end{split}

Finally, using the identity in the last line of (
6.36
) and the bounds (
6.37
),

\langle t\rangle^{3-3/r}\Big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)\sum_{j\in[0,J_{0}]}(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{1,1}_{j,k}(t)\Big\|_{L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{\delta m/3},

for
r\in\{1,\infty\}
. The desired estimates (
6.31
) follow from these last five bounds.
∎

Finally we estimate the remainders arising in the decomposition (
6.28
).

Lemma 6.4
.

With
O^{1,l}_{j,k}
defined as in (
6.30
), for any
(j,k,m)\in A^{II}
and
l\in\{2,3\}
we have

\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)O^{1,l}_{j,k}\|_{Osc_{\delta}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-\delta j}.

(6.43)

Proof.

Recalling the definitions (
6.29
) and the formulas (
3.2
), we compute

\begin{split}&\widetilde{W}^{\prime}_{i,a}(y,v,\tau,s)=-\int_{\tau}^{s}\big[(\gamma-s)\partial_{y^{i}}E^{a}(y-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s),\gamma)\\
&\quad-(\partial_{v^{i}}-s\partial_{y^{i}})\widetilde{Y}^{b}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\partial_{y^{b}}E^{a}(y-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s),\gamma)\big]\,d\gamma\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}_{i,a}(y,v,\tau,s)=\int_{\tau}^{s}\big[(\gamma-\tau)(\gamma-s)\partial_{y^{i}}E^{a}(y-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s),\gamma)\\
&\quad+(\gamma-\tau)(\partial_{v^{i}}-s\partial_{y^{i}})\widetilde{Y}^{b}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\partial_{y^{b}}E^{a}(y-(s-\gamma)v+\widetilde{Y}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s),\gamma)\big]\,d\gamma.\end{split}

Therefore, using (
6.15
), in terms of the trilinear operators defined in (
5.1
) we can write

\begin{split}&O^{1,2}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{b}E^{a},\partial_{a}E^{l};C^{1}_{bl})(x,\gamma,\tau,s;t)\,d\gamma d\tau ds,\\
&C^{1}_{bl}(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s;t):=(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}(\gamma-\tau)E^{i}(y,s)\\
&\qquad\times\big[(\gamma-s)\delta_{i}^{b}+(\partial_{v^{i}}-s\partial_{y^{i}})\widetilde{Y}^{b}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\big]2^{3j}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)),\end{split}

(6.44)

and

\begin{split}&O^{1,3}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\tau}^{s}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{b}E^{a},E^{l};C^{2}_{abl})(x,\gamma,\tau,s;t)\,d\gamma d\tau ds,\\
