&C^{2}_{abl}(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s;t):=(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}E^{i}(y,s)\\
&\qquad\times\big[(\gamma-s)\delta_{i}^{b}+(\partial_{v^{i}}-s\partial_{y^{i}})\widetilde{Y}^{b}(y-sv,v,\gamma,s)\big]2^{3j}\partial_{a}\partial_{l}M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(y-sv,v,\tau,s)).\end{split}

(6.45)

The bounds in Lemma
3.1
(ii) show that

\begin{split}\Lambda(C^{1}_{bl})(\gamma,\tau,s)&\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\langle\gamma\rangle,\\
\Lambda(C^{2}_{abl})(\gamma,\tau,s)&\lesssim(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}.\end{split}

(6.46)

Therefore, using (
5.5
) and (
5.8
), for
l\in\{2,3\}
,

\begin{split}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t){O}^{1,l}_{j,k}(t)\big\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\,d\tau ds\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j}2^{\delta m}\min\{1,2^{-k-m}\}.\end{split}

(6.47)

Moreover, using Sobolev embedding when
s\leq t/2
as before,

\begin{split}\langle t\rangle^{3}&\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t){O}^{1,l}_{j,k}(t)\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{0}^{t/2}\int_{0}^{s}\langle t\rangle^{3}2^{3k}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\,d\tau ds\\
&+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\int_{t/2}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}(1+|t-s|2^{k})^{-8}2^{-j}\langle s\rangle^{-1+\delta}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\,d\tau ds\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j}2^{\delta m}\min\{1,2^{-k-m}\}.\end{split}

(6.48)

Finally, we notice that

\begin{split}&(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|)O^{1,2}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{b}E^{a},\partial_{a}E^{l};C^{1}_{bl})(x,\gamma,\tau,t;t)\,d\gamma d\tau,\\
&(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|)O^{1,3}_{j,k}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\tau}^{t}\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\partial_{b}E^{a},E^{l};C^{2}_{abl})(x,\gamma,\tau,t;t)\,d\gamma d\tau.\end{split}

and one can use (
5.5
) and (
5.8
) again to see that

\langle t\rangle^{3-3/r}\big\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{m}(t)(\partial_{t}+|\nabla_{x}|){O}^{1,l}_{j,k}(t)\big\|_{L^{r}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-j}2^{\delta m}

(6.49)

for
r\in\{1,\infty\}
. The desired bounds (
6.43
) follow from (
6.47
)–(
6.48
) and (
6.49
).
∎

7.
Proof of Lemma
5.1

In this section we prove Lemma
5.1
. The main idea is to decompose the electric field corresponding to the variable
\gamma
into its static and oscillatory parts, and then integrate by parts in
\gamma
in certain cases. Since
\tau,s
are fixed in the proof we may assume that
\Lambda^{\ast}(\tau,s)=1
, thus

|C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)|+|\langle\gamma\rangle\partial_{\gamma}C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)|\lesssim 1\quad\text{ for any }x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s.

(7.1)

7.1.
Proof of (
5.5
)

We may assume that
C
and
\mathcal{K}_{j,k}
are real-valued, decompose
E(\gamma)=E^{stat}(\gamma)+\Re\{e^{-i\gamma}E^{osc}(\gamma)\}
as in (
3.4
), and then decompose dyadically in frequency, so

\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\nabla E,\mathcal{R}^{2}E;C)=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\ast\in\{stat,osc\}}\Re\{\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{R}^{1}(\nabla E),c_{\ast}(\gamma)P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{R}^{2}E^{\ast};C)\},

(7.2)

where
c_{stat}=1
and
c_{osc}=e^{-i\gamma}
. In the following, we set
k_{\min}:=\min\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
.

Step 1.
We bound first the contributions of the static part. Clearly

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|h(y-(s-\mu)v+P(y,v))\big|\,dy\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{1}},

(7.3)

if
h\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and
P
satisfies
|\nabla_{y}P(y,v)|\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}
. Moreover

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\big|h(y-(s-\mu)v+P(y,v))\big|\,dv\lesssim\langle s\rangle^{-3}\|h\|_{L^{1}},

(7.4)

provided that
h\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
\mu\leq s\in[0,T]
satisfy
s-\mu\geq\langle s\rangle/8
, and
|\nabla_{v}P(y,v)|\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle s\rangle
.

As before, let
\rho_{l}:=P_{l}\rho
,
\rho_{l}^{stat}:=P_{l}\rho^{stat}
, and
\rho_{l}^{osc}:=P_{l}\rho^{osc}
. We use Lemma
3.1
(i) and the bounds (
7.1
) to see that

\begin{split}&\|\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat};C)(.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(.,.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\min\{\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat}(\gamma)\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat}(\gamma)\|_{L^{1}}\}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}\cdot 2^{-k_{2}^{+}}\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1+2\delta}\cdot\min\{2^{3k_{\min}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-3},\langle\gamma\rangle^{-3}\},\end{split}

(7.5)

where the operators
\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}
are defined as in (
5.1
). Similarly, using (
7.3
) and (
7.4
) if
s-\gamma\geq\langle s\rangle/8
or
s-\tau\geq\langle s\rangle/8
, together with the observation that
\|\mathcal{K}(.,.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\|\mathcal{K}(.,.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim 1
,

\begin{split}&\|\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat};C)(.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\,\min\big\{\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat}(\gamma)\|_{L^{\infty}},\\
&\quad\qquad\langle s\rangle^{-3}\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat}(\gamma)\|_{L^{1}},\langle s\rangle^{-3}\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}}\|\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat}(\gamma)\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\},\end{split}

and using Lemma
3.1
(i), we see that

\begin{split}&\langle s\rangle^{3}\|\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{stat};C)(.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}2^{-k_{2}^{+}}\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1+2\delta}\min\{2^{k_{\min}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1}\}^{3}.\end{split}

(7.6)

Therefore, using (
5.4
), (
7.5
) and (
7.6
), we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big\{\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{R}^{1}(\nabla E),P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{R}^{2}E^{stat};C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}\\
&\quad+\langle s\rangle^{3}\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}\mathcal{R}^{1}(\nabla E),P_{k_{2}}\mathcal{R}^{2}E^{stat};C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\Big\}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{3\delta m_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta}\min\{2^{-2m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-2}\}.\end{split}

(7.7)

Step 2.
We bound now the contributions of the oscillatory parts, and we will show that

\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{-k_{2}}\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho^{osc}_{k_{2}};C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\}.

(7.8)

Indeed, we estimate first as in (
7.5
)–(
7.6
) to see that for any
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
r\in\{1,\infty\}

\begin{split}&\langle s\rangle^{3-3/r}\|\mathcal{Q}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)(.,\gamma,\tau,s)\|_{L^{r}_{x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}\frac{1}{\langle\gamma\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{2}}+\langle\gamma\rangle^{-\delta}}\min\{2^{k_{\min}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1}\}^{3},\end{split}

(7.9)

where we use (
3.6
) instead of (
3.5
). The bounds (
7.8
) follow if
m_{2}\in[0,400]
or if
\langle\tau\rangle\geq 2^{9m_{2}/8}
. On the other hand, if
m_{2}\geq 400
and
\langle\tau\rangle\leq 2^{9m_{2}/8}
then we have

\begin{split}\sum_{\{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}:\,|k_{2}+m_{2}|\geq m_{2}/8\text{ or }k_{1}\leq-9m_{2}/8\}}2^{-k_{2}}\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\\
\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\}.\end{split}

It remains to bound the contributions coming from indices
k_{2}\in[-9m_{2}/8,-7m_{2}/8]
and
k_{1}\geq-9m_{2}/8
when
m_{2}\geq 400
and
\langle\tau\rangle\leq 2^{9m_{2}/8}
. For this we integrate by parts in
\gamma
. We start by writing

\begin{split}&e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc}(y-(s-\gamma)v+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s),s),\gamma)\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-i\gamma}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\mathcal{R}^{2}(\xi)\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)e^{i\xi\cdot(y-(s-\gamma)v+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s))}\,d\xi.\end{split}

(7.10)

Therefore, using the definitions

\begin{split}\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},&e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)(x,\tau,s)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(x-y,v,\tau,s)\\
&\times C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}})(y-(s-\tau)v+P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s),\tau)\\
&\times\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)e^{i\gamma(-1+\xi\cdot v)}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\mathcal{R}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot(y-sv+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s))}\,d\gamma dydvd\xi.\end{split}

(7.11)

We insert cutoff functions of the form
\varphi_{>p}(1-\xi\cdot v)
and
\varphi_{\leq p}(1-\xi\cdot v)
and use the integration by parts formula

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}e^{i\gamma(-1+\xi\cdot v)}C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)\varphi_{>p}(1-\xi\cdot v)e^{i\xi\cdot(y-sv+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s))}\,d\gamma\\
&=\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}(\partial_{\gamma}C)(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)I^{1}_{>p}(\xi,y,v,\gamma,s)\,d\gamma+\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)I^{2}_{>p}(\xi,y,v,\gamma,s)\,d\gamma\\
&\qquad+\sum_{j\in\{3,4\}}(-1)^{j+1}C(x,y,v,t_{j},\tau,s)I^{1}_{>p}(\xi,y,v,t_{j},s),\end{split}

(7.12)

where

\begin{split}I^{1}_{>p}(\xi,y,v,\gamma,s)&:=\frac{-e^{i\gamma(-1+\xi\cdot v)}}{i(-1+\xi\cdot v)}\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)\varphi_{>p}(1-\xi\cdot v)e^{i\xi\cdot(y-sv+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s))},\\
I^{2}_{>p}(\xi,y,v,\gamma,s)&:=\frac{-e^{i\gamma(-1+\xi\cdot v)}}{i(-1+\xi\cdot v)}\varphi_{>p}(1-\xi\cdot v)e^{i\xi\cdot(y-sv+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s))}\big\{(\partial_{\gamma}\widehat{\rho^{osc}})(\xi,\gamma)\\
&\qquad+\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)i\xi\cdot\partial_{\gamma}P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\big\}.\end{split}

(7.13)

For
p\leq-4
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
let

H^{l}_{>p,k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}I^{l}_{>p}(\xi,y,v,\gamma,s)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\mathcal{R}^{2}(\xi)\,d\xi,

(7.14)

\begin{split}G_{\leq p,k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s):=\frac{e^{-i\gamma}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}&\varphi_{\leq p}(1-\xi\cdot v)\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\\
&\times\mathcal{R}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot(y-(s-\gamma)v+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s))}\,d\xi.\end{split}

(7.15)

In view of these identities, for any
p_{0}\leq-4
we can thus decompose

\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)=X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}+(Y^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}+Y^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}})

(7.16)

where, with
C^{\prime}:=(\partial_{\gamma}C)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{[t_{3},t_{4}]}(\gamma)+C\cdot(\delta_{0}(\gamma-t_{3})-\delta_{0}(\gamma-t_{4}))
,

\begin{split}&X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(x,\tau,s):=\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(x-y,v,\tau,s)C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\\
&\qquad\times(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}})(y-(s-\tau)v+P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s),\tau)G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s)\,dydvd\gamma,\end{split}

(7.17)

\begin{split}&Y^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(x,\tau,s):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(x-y,v,\tau,s)C^{\prime}(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\\
&\qquad\times(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}})(y-(s-\tau)v+P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s),\tau)H^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s)\,dydvd\gamma,\end{split}

(7.18)

\begin{split}&Y^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(x,\tau,s):=\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}_{j,k}(x-y,v,\tau,s)C(x,y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)\\
&\qquad\times(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}})(y-(s-\tau)v+P_{1}(y,v,\tau,s),\tau)H^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s)\,dydvd\gamma.\end{split}

(7.19)

Step 3.
We will now prove bounds on the functions
H^{l}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}
and
G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}
. We claim that

\begin{split}&\|H^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{1}_{y}}+\langle\gamma\rangle^{3}\|H^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-p_{0}}\langle\gamma\rangle^{\delta},\\
&\|H^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{1}_{y}}+\langle\gamma\rangle^{3}\|H^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{-p_{0}}\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1+2\delta},\end{split}

(7.20)

and

\begin{split}\|G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{p_{0}}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1+2\delta}2^{k_{2}},\\
\|G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{1}_{y}}+\langle\gamma\rangle^{3}\|G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle\gamma\rangle^{\delta},\end{split}

(7.21)

for any
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Indeed, we examine the definitions (
7.13
)–(
7.14
) and rewrite

\begin{split}H^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s)&=-e^{-i\gamma}\widetilde{H}^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(y-(s-\gamma)v+P_{2}(y,v,\gamma,\tau,s)),\\
\widetilde{H}^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(z,v,\gamma)&:=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\varphi_{k_{2}}(\xi)\mathcal{R}^{2}(\xi)\frac{\varphi_{>p_{0}}(1-\xi\cdot v)}{i(-1+\xi\cdot v)}\widehat{\rho^{osc}}(\xi,\gamma)e^{i\xi\cdot z}\,d\xi.\end{split}

It follows from Lemma
3.3
(ii) that

\|\widetilde{H}^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,v,\gamma)\|_{L^{q}_{z}}\lesssim 2^{-p_{0}}\|\rho^{osc}(.,\gamma)\|_{L^{q}},\qquad q\in[1,\infty],

and the desired bounds in the first line of (
7.20
) follow using (
3.6
) and (
7.3
). The bounds on
H^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}
in the second line of (
7.20
) follow by a similar argument.

To prove the bounds (
7.21
) we examine the formula (
7.15
) and notice that if
p_{0},k_{2}\leq 4
then the functions
G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}
are nontrivial only if
|v|\geq 1
. Using the bounds (
3.19
)

|G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(y,v,\gamma,s)|\lesssim 2^{2k_{2}}2^{p_{0}}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\|P_{k_{2}}\rho^{osc}(.,\gamma)\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}2^{p_{0}}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\langle\gamma\rangle^{-1+2\delta}2^{k_{2}},

for any
y,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, as claimed. The remaining bounds in (
7.21
) follow from the corresponding bounds on
\rho(.,\gamma)
and Lemma
3.3
(i).

Step 4.
We return now to the proof of the main bounds (
7.8
). In view of the discussion in
Step 2
, it suffices to show that if
m_{2}\geq 400
and
\langle\tau\rangle\leq 2^{9m_{2}/8}
then

\begin{split}\sum_{\{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}:\,|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8\text{ and }k_{1}\geq-9m_{2}/8\}}2^{-k_{2}}\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\\
\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\}.\end{split}

(7.22)

Recall that
\|\mathcal{K}(.,.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}+\|\mathcal{K}(.,.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{\infty}_{v}}\lesssim 1
(see (
5.2
)). We use the decomposition (
7.16
) with
p_{0}:=-m_{2}/2
. The function
X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}
defined in (
7.17
) is nontrivial only if
k_{2}+j\geq-20
. Using (
7.1
) and (
7.21
), we have

\begin{split}\|X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&\lesssim\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}}\|G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(.,.,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,v}}\,d\gamma\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{2\delta m_{2}}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}2^{p_{0}}2^{-j}2^{k_{2}}.\end{split}

(7.23)

Using the trivial estimate if
s-\tau\leq\langle s\rangle/8
and (
7.4
) if
s-\tau\geq\langle s\rangle/8
, together with (
7.21
),

\begin{split}\|X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}&\lesssim\int_{t_{3}}^{t_{4}}\|G_{\leq p_{0},k_{2}}(.,.,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,v}}\cdot\langle s\rangle^{-3}\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\tau}}\,\,d\gamma\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{2\delta m_{2}}2^{p_{0}-j+k_{2}}\cdot\langle s\rangle^{-3}\frac{1}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}.\end{split}

(7.24)

Therefore, recalling that
-j\leq 20+k_{2}
and
\langle\tau\rangle\leq 2^{9m_{2}/8}
, we have

\sum_{\{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}:\,|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8\text{ and }k_{1}\geq-9m_{2}/8\}}2^{-k_{2}}\|X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-5m_{2}/4}.

(7.25)

Now, we proceed to estimate the contributions of the function
Y^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(x,\tau,s)
defined in (
7.18
). As before, we use (
7.1
) and (
7.20
) to estimate, for
p_{0}=-m_{2}/2
,

\begin{split}\|Y^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{1}_{x}}&\lesssim\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}}\|H^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,.,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,v}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}2^{-p_{0}}2^{2\delta m_{2}}2^{-3m_{2}}.\end{split}

(7.26)

Moreover, using also (
7.4
) if
s-\tau\geq\langle s\rangle/8
and a direct
L^{\infty}
bound if
s-\tau\leq\langle s\rangle/8
, we have

\begin{split}\|Y^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}&\lesssim\|H^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{2}}(.,.,\gamma,s)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y,v}}\cdot\langle s\rangle^{-3}\|\rho_{k_{1}}(\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\tau}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle s\rangle^{-3}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}2^{-p_{0}}2^{\delta m_{2}}2^{-3m_{2}}.\end{split}

(7.27)

Therefore, after combining the obtained estimates (
7.26
) and (
7.27
), we have

\sum_{\{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}:\,|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8\text{ and }k_{1}\geq-9m_{2}/8\}}2^{-k_{2}}\|Y^{1}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-5m_{2}/4}.

The contributions of the functions
Y^{2}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}
can be estimated in a similar way, as there is an additional factor of
2^{m_{2}}
(since
C^{\prime}
is replaced with
C
) but also a factor of
2^{-m_{2}}
in (
7.20
). The bounds (
7.22
) follow using also (
7.25
) and recalling that
\langle\tau\rangle^{-1}\geq 2^{-9m_{2}/8}
. This completes the proof of the main bounds (
5.5
).

7.2.
Proof of (
5.6
)

Step 1.
As in subsection
7.1
we decompose

\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(E,\nabla E;C)=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\ast\in\{stat,osc\}}\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}E,c_{\ast}(\gamma)P_{k_{2}}(\nabla E^{\ast});C),

(7.28)

where
c_{stat}=1
and
c_{osc}=e^{-i\gamma}
as before. As in (
7.5
)–(
7.6
), for
r\in\{1,\infty\}
we have

\begin{split}\langle s\rangle^{3-3/r}&\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}E,P_{k_{2}}(\nabla E^{stat});C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{r}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-k_{1}}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}2^{-k_{2}^{+}}2^{2\delta m_{2}}\min\{2^{k_{\min}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},2^{-m_{2}}\}^{3}\end{split}

(7.29)

and, as in (
7.9
),

\begin{split}\langle s\rangle^{3-3/r}&\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(P_{k_{1}}E,e^{-i\gamma}P_{k_{2}}(\nabla E^{osc});C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{L^{r}_{x}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-k_{1}}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}\frac{2^{m_{2}}}{2^{(1-2\delta)m_{2}}2^{k_{2}}+2^{-\delta m_{2}}}\min\{2^{k_{\min}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},2^{-m_{2}}\}^{3}\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta}2^{2\delta m_{2}}\min\{\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},2^{-m_{2}}\}\frac{\min\{1,2^{m_{2}+k_{2}},2^{m_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-1},2^{m_{2}+k_{1}}\}}{(1+2^{k_{2}+m_{2}})(1+\langle\tau\rangle 2^{k_{1}})}.\end{split}

(7.30)

These bounds are sufficient to estimate the contributions of the static component
E^{stat}
and of the oscillatory component
E^{osc}
when
2^{9m_{2}/8}\lesssim\langle\tau\rangle
. They also suffice to estimate the contributions corresponding to frequencies
\{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}:\,|k_{2}+m_{2}|\geq m_{2}/8\text{ or }k_{1}\leq-9m_{2}/8\text{ or }k_{1}\leq k_{2}-m/8\}
in the oscillatory component
E^{osc}
.

Step 2.
As before, it remains to prove that if
m_{2}\geq 400
and
2^{9m_{2}/8}\geq\langle\tau\rangle
then

\begin{split}&\sum_{|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8,\,k_{1}\geq-m_{2}/8+\max(k_{2},-m_{2})}2^{-k_{1}}\|\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\min\{2^{-1.1m_{2}},\langle\tau\rangle^{-1.1}\},\end{split}

(7.31)

where
\mathcal{R}^{1},\mathcal{R}^{2}
are operators defined by multipliers satisfying the differential inequalities (
5.11
).

We use the identities (
7.10
)–(
7.11
), integrate by parts as in (
7.12
)–(
7.13
), and decompose
\mathcal{B}_{j,k}(\mathcal{R}^{1}\rho_{k_{1}},e^{-i\gamma}\mathcal{R}^{2}\rho_{k_{2}}^{osc};C)
as in (
7.14
)–(
7.19
) with
p_{0}=-m_{2}/2
. Then we use the bounds (
7.23
)–(
7.24
) and recall that the functions
X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}
are nontrivial only if
-j\leq 20+k_{2}
, so

\begin{split}&\sum_{|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8,\,k_{1}\geq-m_{2}/8+\max(k_{2},-m_{2})}2^{-k_{1}}\|X_{\leq p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\sum_{|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8,\,k_{1}\geq-m_{2}/8+\max(k_{2},-m_{2})}2^{-k_{1}}\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{2\delta m_{2}}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}2^{p_{0}}2^{2k_{2}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{3\delta m_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{3\delta}2^{-5m_{2}/4}.\end{split}

(7.32)

Similarly, using (
7.26
)–(
7.27
), for
l\in\{1,2\}
we estimate

\begin{split}&\sum_{|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8,\,k_{1}\geq-m_{2}/8+\max(k_{2},-m_{2})}2^{-k_{1}}\|Y^{l}_{>p_{0},k_{1},k_{2}}(.,\tau,s)\|_{B^{0}_{s}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\sum_{|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8,\,k_{1}\geq-m_{2}/8+\max(k_{2},-m_{2})}2^{-k_{1}}\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{-p_{0}}2^{-m_{2}(3-2\delta)}}{\langle\tau\rangle^{1-2\delta}2^{k_{1}}+\langle\tau\rangle^{-\delta}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}^{2}2^{3\delta m_{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{3\delta}2^{-5m_{2}/4}.\end{split}

(7.33)

The desired bounds (
7.31
) follow since
2^{9m_{2}/8}\geq\langle\tau\rangle
.

7.3.
Proofs of (
5.7
)–(
5.10
)

These estimates can be proved in the same way. We first decompose the electric field in the second position into its static and oscillatory components, and decompose dyadically in frequency. Then we estimate the contributions of the static components using bounds similar to (
7.29
) (with different factors of
2^{-k_{1}}
or
2^{-k_{2}}
) and the contributions of most of the oscillatory components using bounds similar to (
7.30
).

After these reductions we are left with the contributions of the oscillatory components when
m_{2}\geq 400
and
2^{9m_{2}/8}\geq\langle\tau\rangle
, coming from frequencies
\{k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}:\,|k_{2}+m_{2}|\leq m_{2}/8\text{ and }k_{1}\geq-m_{2}/8+\max\{-m_{2},k_{2}\}\}
. Here we integrate by parts in
\gamma
, as before, and use the bounds (
7.23
)–(
7.24
) and (
7.26
)–(
7.27
) to control the corresponding contributions. To get bounds similar to (
7.32
)–(
7.33
) we need to use different cutoff values of
p_{0}
: we set
p_{0}=-3m_{2}/8
for the bounds (
5.7
) and
p_{0}=-m/2
for the bounds (
5.8
)–(
5.10
) (the frequency
k_{1}
is fixed in the last two bounds, in order to avoid the divergent sum corresponding to
k_{1}\geq 0
, and the sum in the second step is simply taken over frequencies
k_{2}\in[-9m_{2}/8,-7m_{2}/8]
).

8.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section we show that Proposition
2.4
implies the main result, Theorem
1.1
.

Proof of global existence and uniqueness.

Let
\overline{\varepsilon}>0
and
\delta>0
as in Proposition
2.4
be given, and let
f_{0}
satisfying the assumptions (
1.6
) (resp. (
2.40
)) be given. Then by standard local existence theory we can find
T>0
and a unique solution
f\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[0,T])
to (
1.4
), (
1.5
). Moreover, by Corollary
2.3
we can decompose the associated density
\rho(x,t)
as in (
2.36
), and by continuity (note that
\rho^{osc}(x,0)=0
) we can assume that
T>0
is chosen such that the smallness assumption (
2.41
) of Proposition
2.4
is met.

Noting that
f
can be recovered exactly by integrating along the characteristics, i.e. that by (
2.4
) we have

\begin{split}f(x,v,t)=f_{0}(X(x,v,0,t),V(x,v,0,t))+\left[M_{0}(V(x,v,0,t))-M_{0}(v)\right],\end{split}

(8.1)

and the characteristics in turn are determined by
\rho
and its derivative through the system of ODEs (
2.3
), we can apply the conclusion (
2.42
) of Proposition
2.4
to extend the solution
f
to a larger time interval
[0,T^{\prime}]
with
T^{\prime}>T
, on which the splitting of the density according to Corollary
2.3
and the corresponding smallness condition (
2.41
) still hold. Hence we obtain a unique global solution
f\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+})
with associated density
\rho=\rho^{stat}+\Re\{e^{-it}\rho^{osc}\}
satisfying for all
t>0
that

\left\|\rho^{stat}\right\|_{Stat_{\delta}}+\left\|\rho^{osc}\right\|_{Osc_{\delta}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{0}.

(8.2)

The corresponding splitting (
1.8
) of the electric field follows directly by (
3.4
).
∎

We prove next the scattering statement (
1.7
) of Theorem
1.1
:

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume
f
is a global solution as constructed above, so that in particular the associated density satisfies (
8.2
). Then there exists
f_{\infty}\in L^{\infty}_{x,v}
such that

\begin{split}\|f(x,v,t)-f_{\infty}(x-tv,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(8.3)

Remark 8.2
.

Using formula (
8.1
) and with additional simple work on the characteristics, one could obtain the formula

\begin{split}f_{\infty}(x,v):=(f_{0}\circ\Phi)(x,v)+\left[M_{0}(\Phi^{2}(x,v))-M_{0}(v)\right]\end{split}

where
\Phi=(\Phi^{1},\Phi^{2})
is a
C^{1}
symplectic diffeomorphism.

Proposition
8.1
essentially follows from the following lemma:

Lemma 8.3
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
8.1
, there exist
(\widetilde{Y}_{\infty},\widetilde{W}_{\infty}):\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that for each fixed
s
, we have that

\begin{split}\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\left[|\widetilde{Y}(x,v,s,t)-\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(x,v;s)|+|\widetilde{W}(x,v,s,t)-\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x,v;s)|\right]\lesssim\varepsilon_{1},\end{split}

(8.4)

uniformly in
0\leq s\leq t<\infty
.

We first show how Proposition
8.1
follows from Lemma
8.3
.

Proof of Proposition
8.1
assuming Lemma
8.3
.

Write for simplicity
\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(x,v):=\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(x,v;0)
and
\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x,v):=\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x,v;0)
and let

\begin{split}G(y,u)&:=f_{0}(y+\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(y,u),u+\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(y,u))+\left[M_{0}(u+\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(y,u))-M_{0}(u)\right]\\
\end{split}

be the (putative) scattering data from Lemma
8.3
. By (
8.1
) we can rewrite

\begin{split}f(x,v,t)&-G(x-tv,v)=f_{0}(x-tv+\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,0,t),v+\widetilde{W}(x-tv,v,0,t))\\
&-f_{0}(x-tv+\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(x-tv,v),v+\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x-tv,v))\\
&+M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}(x-tv,v,0,t))-M_{0}(v+\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x-tv,v))\\
&=\delta Y(x,v,t)\cdot A_{x}(x,v,t)+\delta W(x,v,t)\cdot A_{v}(x,v,t)+\delta W(x,v,t)\cdot B(x,v,t),\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\delta Y

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,0,t)-\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(x-tv,v),

\displaystyle Y_{0}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{Y}(x-tv,v,0,t),

\displaystyle Y_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}(x-tv,v)

\displaystyle\delta W

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{W}(x-tv,v,0,t)-\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x-tv,v),

\displaystyle W_{0}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{W}(x-tv,v,0,t),

\displaystyle W_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=\widetilde{W}_{\infty}(x-tv,v)

and

\displaystyle A_{x}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\nabla_{x}f_{0}(x-tv+\theta Y_{0}+(1-\theta)Y_{\infty},v+\theta W_{0}+(1-\theta)W_{\infty})d\theta,

\displaystyle A_{v}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\nabla_{v}f_{0}(x-tv+\theta Y_{0}+(1-\theta)Y_{\infty},v+\theta W_{0}+(1-\theta)W_{\infty})d\theta,

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\int_{\theta=0}^{1}\nabla_{v}M_{0}(v+\theta W_{0}+(1-\theta)W_{\infty})d\theta.

By our choice of background
M_{0}
in (
1.5
) and the assumptions on the initial data (
2.40
), together with the estimate (
3.52
) in Lemma
3.8
, which ensures that
\langle v\rangle\sim\langle v+\widetilde{W}\rangle,
we directly see that

\begin{split}\|\langle v\rangle A_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle A_{v}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}&\lesssim\varepsilon_{0},\qquad\|\langle v\rangle B\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\lesssim 1.\end{split}

Letting
f_{\infty}:=G
, together with the bound (
8.4
) for
\delta Y
and
\delta W
the claim follows.
∎

It remains to prove Lemma
8.3
.

Proof of Lemma
8.3
.

Given
0\leq s\leq t\leq t^{\prime}
, we can write

\begin{split}\Delta_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)&:=\widetilde{Y}(x,v,s,t^{\prime})-\widetilde{Y}(x,v,s,t)\\
&=\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}(\tau-s)E(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)d\tau\\
&\quad+\int_{\tau=s}^{t}(\tau-s)\left[E(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)-E(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t),\tau)\right]d\tau\\
&=\mathfrak{e}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)+\mathfrak{r}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v).\end{split}

A crude estimate gives

\begin{split}|\mathfrak{r}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)|&\lesssim\int_{\tau=s}^{t}\tau\|\nabla_{x}E(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\cdot|\Delta_{\tau,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)|d\tau\end{split}

and observing that

g(\tau):=\tau\|\nabla_{x}E(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-2}

belongs to
L^{1}_{\tau}
and using Grönwall’s Lemma, we see that it suffices to bound
\mathfrak{e}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)
by
\varepsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}}
to prove the claim for
\widetilde{Y}-\widetilde{Y}_{\infty}
. To obtain this, we can decompose

\begin{split}\mathfrak{e}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)&=\mathfrak{e}^{stat}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)+\Re(\mathfrak{e}^{osc}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)),\\
\mathfrak{e}^{(\ast)}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)&=\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}(\tau-s)E^{\ast}(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

On the one hand, we see that

\begin{split}|\mathfrak{e}^{stat}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)|&\lesssim\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}\tau\|E^{stat}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-2}d\tau\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{2\delta-1}.\end{split}

We now consider

\begin{split}\mathfrak{e}^{osc}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)&=\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}(\tau-s)e^{-i\tau}E^{osc}(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)d\tau\end{split}

and do a direct normal form transformation to get

\begin{split}\mathfrak{e}^{osc}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)&=\left[i(\tau-s)e^{-i\tau}E^{osc}(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)\right]_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}\\
&\quad-i\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-i\tau}E^{osc}(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)d\tau\\
&\quad-i\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-i\tau}(\tau-s)\partial_{\tau}E^{osc}(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)d\tau\\
&\quad-i\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-i\tau}(\tau-s)\left((v^{j}+\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{Y}^{j}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}))\partial_{x^{j}}\right)E^{osc}(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

Another crude integration gives that

\begin{split}|\mathfrak{e}^{osc}_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)|&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2\delta-1}.\end{split}

To sum up, we have proved that

\big|\Delta_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)\big|\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2\delta-1}.

(8.5)

We can control the
\widetilde{W}
variation using a similar decomposition and the above obtained estimate of
\Delta_{s,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)
:

\begin{split}|\widetilde{W}(x,v,s,t^{\prime})-&\widetilde{W}(x,v,s,t)|\leq\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}\left|E(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)\right|d\tau\\
&+\int_{\tau=s}^{t}\left|E(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t^{\prime}),\tau)-E(x+\tau v+\widetilde{Y}(x,v,\tau,t),\tau)\right|d\tau\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\int_{\tau=t}^{t^{\prime}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-2}d\tau+\int_{\tau=s}^{t}\|\nabla_{x}E(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}\cdot|\Delta_{\tau,t,t^{\prime}}(x,v)|d\tau\\
&\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle t\rangle^{2\delta-1}+\varepsilon_{1}^{2}\langle v\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2\delta-1}\int_{\tau=s}^{t}\langle\tau\rangle^{2\delta-3}d\tau\lesssim\varepsilon_{1}\langle v\rangle\langle t\rangle^{2\delta-1}.\end{split}

Hence finishing the proof of Lemma
8.3
.
∎
